## §249 — CLOSING THE "WEEK WITH ROOM LEFT" GAP: A NEW BID ON A COMPETITION WEEK MUST MATCH THE LOWEST WINNING BID ("NO WEAKENING AT ALL" RULED, THEN WITHDRAWN) — 7 Oct 2026

**How it came up.** With staff 194 filed and not yet pushed, he wrote, verbatim: *"I'm interested in closing the \"week with room left\" gap. can that happen here or maybe in a new session?"* — the gap he had accepted in §245: on a competition week with an open slot a newcomer may take the slot with any number, and the rule (a winner may come down to the lowest other winning bid) then lets every winner come down to it. Claude gave the context reading (567,267; the 194 cycle took about 300,000) and the question that decides the rule — what a winner's bid is measured against when a slot is open — with two answers: no weakening at all (simplest, closes it completely, but ends his own "winners at 1, 2, 3 and 5 may come down to 5" example of §245), or only winning bids that were there during the competition (keeps the example, more code).

**His ruling, verbatim: *"Nothing: no weakening at all on a competition week."***

**The rule.** On a week where a bidding competition has happened this phase (§245's definition, unchanged), a bid may not be made weaker — whatever it would be projected. Stronger bids, the same bid, first bids and removals are untouched; weeks with no competition are untouched. This supersedes §248's "only if it would still win, no lower than the lowest other winning bid" and §245's "equal to the weakest other winning bid is allowed".

**What it changes beyond the one check (put to him, wording NOT yet ruled):** the refusal sentence, the Rules & Reminders line and the bid-box note of §248 each promise "a weaker bid that would still win" and must be reworded; the alert e-mail's "lowest bid of yours that would also win here" sentence can no longer name a bid on a competition week, so that line stops being sent there (an e-mail change, unlike 194).

**Status: RULED, NOT PLANNED, NOT BUILT (§92). Staff 194 (§248) went live 7 Oct about 7:48 AM (`e154617`) — he pushed it straight after this ruling; this is a further build (195), to be planned. Wordings proposed to him and not yet ruled: refusal *"On a week with a bidding competition, a bid can't be weakened."*; Rules & Reminders *"On a week where more people have bid this phase than it can hold, you can't weaken your bid. You can always strengthen or remove a bid."*; bid-box note *"No bid is available to you on this week right now. On a week with a bidding competition you can bid again only with your previous bid or stronger. If your previous bid number is on another week, moving it back makes it available here."*; and the alert e-mail's "lowest bid of yours that would also win here" sentence no longer sent for a competition week.**

**RECONSIDERED THE SAME MORNING — §249 IS NOT SETTLED.** Ten minutes after the ruling (and after pushing 194) he quoted back Claude's sentence that the rule ends "winners at 1, 2, 3 and 5 may each come down to 5", with no instruction. Claude corrected its own sentence (Tuesday night, not Monday; the example was Claude's description, which he confirmed — not his own), set out the case in a table, and asked which he meant. **His answer, verbatim: *"this is a problem"*.** So "no weakening at all" as worded costs something he wants kept. What he wants, as Claude reads it and put back to him (NOT yet confirmed): winners on a competition week may still come down to the lowest winning bid that came out of the competition, but a weak bid placed later into an empty slot must not become a level others can drop to. The definition of "came out of the competition" is the open design question for the plan. **Nothing is to be built from the "no weakening at all" wording.**

**HIS OWN IDEA, 7 Oct 2026 about 8:12 AM, verbatim: *"Why can't each person lower to a 5 and a new person would have to come in with a 5?"*** — i.e. keep 194's come-down to the lowest winning bid, and close the room-left gap at the door: a NEW bid on a competition week must be at least as strong as the lowest winning bid there. Claude: it can work and is easier to explain than ignoring a newcomer's bid afterwards; two things it needs him to decide — (a) whether a new bid that would LOSE may still be weaker than that level (he said on 6 Oct *"anyone can place a losing bid at any time"*; if it may, a weak losing bid can wait for someone to leave and then become the level), and (b) what the entry level is when every winner has left and no winning bid remains. NOT ruled; nothing planned or built.

**RULED, 7 Oct 2026 about 8:24 AM, verbatim: *"1-agree. 2-if everyone has left, then anything goes. but only if everyone has left."*** — on Claude's two questions: (1) the entry rule applies to EVERY new bid on a competition week, winning or not (this replaces, for competition weeks, his 6 Oct *"anyone can place a losing bid at any time"*); (2) when everyone has left the week there is no entry level — any bid may be placed — and ONLY then.

**THE RULE AS IT NOW STANDS (§249, final form; replaces the "no weakening at all" wording above, which is withdrawn):** on a week where a bidding competition has happened this phase —
· **coming down (unchanged, staff 194, §248):** a bid may be weakened only if it would still win and is no lower than the lowest other winning bid;
· **coming in (NEW):** a new bid must be at least as strong as the lowest winning bid on that week, whether or not it would win;
· **the exception:** if everyone has left the week, any bid may be placed.
So the lowest winning bid on a competition week cannot be undercut by a later arrival, and the "week with room left" gap of §245 is closed at the door.

**Open for the plan (put to him, not ruled):** (a) the entry level when bids remain but NOBODY is projected to win (a tie shown as Under Review); (b) whether "anything goes" on an emptied week also frees someone who left it earlier (194 holds a returning bidder to their previous bid); (c) the wording — refusal sentence, Rules & Reminders, the greyed numbers in a newcomer's box.

**Status: RULED; NOT PLANNED, NOT BUILT (§92). A further staff build (195), for a new session.**

**ON THE THREE OPEN POINTS, 7 Oct 2026 about 8:27 AM, verbatim: *"1-ok if a new bid is then allowed to come in as under review? 2-also frees for newcomer, but uses bid lowering. 3-make suggestions."*** Claude's reading, put back to him for confirmation (NOT yet confirmed): **(a)** when bids remain but nobody is projected to win, the entry level is the bid of those Under Review — a new bid may match it and join the review (Claude: yes, that is consistent; with a winner present the level stays the lowest WINNING bid, so a bid that would only tie people already Under Review behind the winners is refused). **(b)** on an emptied week the person who left earlier is freed as well — but a bid weaker than their previous one costs a bid lowering, exactly as the ordinary lowering rule charges it (so on an emptied week the competition rule simply stands aside and the ordinary rules apply to everyone). **(c)** wording suggested in the chat: coming-in refusal *"On a week with a bidding competition, a new bid must be at least as strong as the lowest winning bid on that week."*; Rules & Reminders, one line for both directions *"On a week where more people have bid this phase than it can hold, a new bid must be at least as strong as the lowest winning bid, and you can only weaken your bid if it would still win and is no lower than the lowest other winning bid. You can always strengthen or remove a bid."*; the bid-box note for a newcomer with every number greyed *"No bid is available to you on this week right now. On a week with a bidding competition a new bid must be at least as strong as the lowest winning bid."* None approved yet.

**CONFIRMED, 7 Oct 2026 about 8:29 AM, verbatim: *"1- can we allow the 8? 2-yes. 3-good. ready for handoff?"*** — **(a)** on Claude's example (winners down to a 7, two 8s Under Review: a newcomer would need a 7; an 8 refused) he asked for the 8 to be allowed; Claude: yes — so **the entry level is the weakest bid on that week that is projected to WIN or is UNDER REVIEW** (a new bid may match it and join the review); it does not loosen coming down, which still requires the weaker bid to WIN at or above the lowest other WINNING bid. One consequence told to him: with a wide review threshold a trailing bid can show as Under Review and so set a weaker entry level; a bid that enters that way is not a winning bid, so nobody can come down to it unless winners later leave and it moves up — the case he already knows. **(b)** confirmed: on an emptied week the person who left earlier is freed as well, and a bid weaker than their previous one costs a bid lowering — the competition rule stands aside and the ordinary rules apply. **(c)** the wording *"good"* — approved as suggested, with ONE change that (a) forces and that he has NOT yet seen: "the lowest winning bid" in the coming-in sentence, the Rules & Reminders line and the newcomer's note must read "the weakest bid that is winning or Under Review" (or his choice of words) — put to him in the plan.

**Status: RULED in full; NOT PLANNED, NOT BUILT (§92). Staff 195, new session: explore the code, then ONE plan.**

**THE PLAN'S FOUR POINTS, 7 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 7 Oct 2026 V1"), about 9:00 AM — plan `tests/docs/PLAN-195-ENTRY-LEVEL-ON-A-COMPETITION-WEEK-2026-10-07.md`. His answers, verbatim: *"1-agree. 2-keep my ruling. in this situation the bidder almost certainly overbid on accident and can lower. it's exceedingly unlikely that all bidders would leave a week that had a bidding war. Am i thinking about this right? 3-agree, no parked losing bids unless that bid was previously winning or under review. that work? 4-ok"*** — **(1) RULED:** a returning bidder's bid is a new bid and must meet the entry level. **(3) RULED:** the bid that starts the competition is held to the level (the week is judged as it will be once the bid lands); a bid that came in at or above the level and was later outbid stays on the week — Claude's reading of his "unless", put back to him. **(4) RULED:** with bids remaining but none winning or Under Review, the level is the strongest bid still there. **(2) NOT SETTLED — he asked whether he is thinking about it right; Claude's answer: the case is not "everyone left" but "everyone but one left", and in Phase 2 and later, with earlier-phase winners holding most of a week, one open slot fought over by two or three people is the ordinary competition week — the last one there removes and re-bids weaker for one lowering, which is the 6 Oct problem. Options put to him: A as ruled; B the freedom only for people who have not bid there this phase; C (recommended) the person who left EARLIER is freed, the person whose own removal emptied the week is not. Awaiting his word. Nothing built (§92).**

**About 9:04 AM, verbatim: *"wordings good. accept greyed numbers. point 2 - what do you recommend?"*** — **RULED:** the three wordings of the plan's §8 (with "winning or under review") are approved; the greyed numbers showing anyone the entry level of a competition week are accepted. **Point 2 still open:** Claude recommended C again; his choice awaited. No go on the build yet (§92).

**POINT 2 RULED, about 9:09 AM, verbatim: *"I actually think A"*** — after Claude recommended C twice and set out the cost (in Phase 2, with one slot open, the last bidder on a competition week can remove and re-bid weaker for one lowering — what 194 refuses today). **A stands: when everyone has left a competition week the competition rule stands aside for everyone, including someone who bid there earlier and including the person whose own removal emptied it; a bid weaker than their previous one costs a bid lowering, as the ordinary rule charges it.** His reasons, verbatim (9:00 AM): *"in this situation the bidder almost certainly overbid on accident and can lower. it's exceedingly unlikely that all bidders would leave a week that had a bidding war."* Accepted with the cost known; not to be re-raised. **All four points, the wording and the greyed numbers are now ruled. His go on the build: NOT yet given (§92).**

**HIS GO ON THE BUILD, 7 Oct 2026 about 9:17 AM, verbatim: *"go"*** — staff 195, built outside the repo folder from the live 194 (`e154617`, `857cf83d…`), written in only when gated and audited.

**AFTER THE BUILD AND ITS BLIND AUDIT, 7 Oct 2026 about 11:26 AM (195 built in scratch, NOT filed).** The audit's H1 (the Remove box still promises a re-bid that 195 refuses — a winner who removes to swap a number cannot put the same bid back when it is below the level) and M1 (in Phase 4, Round 2 and later, the competition record runs from the phase's start) were put to him with options A / B / C. **His answer, verbatim: *"if the user was winning when they left and then their same bid would be winning when they come back, are you sure that's a problem? Each round of phase 4 must follow the same rules and I want that done now."*** — **(i) RULED: each Phase-4 round follows the same rules — the competition record is the ROUND's, not the phase's; to be done in this build.** Read in the code: Start Round already clears the floors and the lowerings and removes the denied bids; only the change-log window in `_wwContested` still runs from the phase's reset — it becomes the round's own stamp (`p4RoundBeganAt`), as `_lwReturns` already reads it. **(ii) His question — Claude's answer: no, that case is not a problem** (a bid that was winning when it left and would win again restores a level the week already had; it is not a later arrival undercutting anyone). If you remove this bid, a new bid here must be at least as strong as the weakest bid there that is winning or under review — you may not be able to place this same bid again.""*** — **(a) THE RETURNING WINNER:** someone whose bid was projected WIN when they removed it may put back that same bid, or a stronger one, below the entry level, provided it would be projected WIN again; anything else from them is a new bid. The second audit showed that recording every winning removal let a win be banked: bid a 10 on a quiet week, remove it at once, and use the record after a competition had come and gone to enter below the level. His words were *"if the user was winning when they left"*, said of a competition week; Claude built the narrower reading rather than the hole. (2) **in a Phase-4 round with no stamp (abnormal data) there is no way back**, as `_lwReturns` already behaves. **HIS CALL, put to him at filing, NOT built — R1:** a win can still be banked on an EMPTIED week that counts as a competition week only once the person's own bid is on it (others' history, the week empty, bid, remove, return below the level later); it needs other people's history adding up to the week's capacity and one person cannot set it up alone; the tightening would be "a removal that empties the week records no win". **Also told:** the greyed numbers reveal nothing new (the week card already shows every bid — Claude's earlier statement to him was wrong and was corrected the same day); removals made before 195 carry no record of a win; a 194 tab still open records none either.

## §251 — A BIDDING COMPETITION MEANS MORE ON THE WEEK THAN IT CAN HOLD AT THE SAME TIME; "PREVIOUS PHASES DON'T MATTER" — 7 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 7 Oct 2026 V2", about 7:00–7:02 PM.** Reading the proposed Rules & Reminders definition he asked, verbatim: *"is this only if it's more people all at the same time?  does it count if a few people come and go, but the total never exceeds the availability?"* Claude's answer from live staff 195 (`_wwContested`): it is NOT at the same time — everyone who has bid on the week in the phase is added together (by FTE, each person once, plus earlier-phase holders), so people who came and went and never overlapped do count; that strictness was in the description he accepted on 6 Oct (§245, *"I like it."*). **HIS RULING, verbatim: *"I need at the same time and previous phases don't matter"*.** This CHANGES the definition accepted in §245: a competition has happened on a week only when, at one moment in the phase (in Phase 4, the round), the people with a bid on it together came to more than it can hold. **"Previous phases don't matter" is NOT yet pinned down — asked of him the same turn:** (a) a competition in an earlier phase does not carry into this one (already so), or (b) people who WON the week in an earlier phase are not counted against its capacity (Claude advised against (b): a week with 3 of 5 FTE already won has room for 2, and three people bidding for those 2 at once is a competition). **Status: read-only exploration only. The live rule (193–195) rests on `_wwContested`; changing it is its own staff build, to be planned, gated and audited before the label (§250), which shows whatever the definition says. NOT planned in full, NOT approved to build, nothing built (§92).** Read so far: the phase's change log (`vacations/changes`) carries every bidder's added / edited / lowered / removed entry with its time and is reset only at a phase close, so "who was on the week together" can be replayed from it with the live board as the end state; open for the plan — admin-made entries and removals, simulator bids (no log entry), a log entry that failed to write, FTE edits mid-phase, and which way the test answers when it cannot tell.
**RULED about 7:03 PM, verbatim: *"I want A"*** — a competition in an earlier phase does not carry into this one; FTE won on the week in an earlier phase still takes up its room; only this phase's (in Phase 4, this round's) bidders, there at the same time, make a competition. Explored and planned the same turn as Part 0 of `tests/docs/PLAN-196-COMPETITION-LABEL-12H-RULES-2026-10-07.md` (the body of `_wwContested` only; replay of the phase's change log). Claude changed its advice on the order and said so: one build with the label, the 12-hour change and the rules lines rather than the definition first, because the Rules & Reminders sentence must change at the same moment as the definition. NOT approved to build, nothing built (§92).
**Wording of the definition line, his, about 7:08 PM, verbatim: change *"it can hold, at any point this phase (in Phase 4, this round). It stays one after some of them leave."* to *"it has available, at any point this phase. The remains a competition week for the duration of the phase."*** — taken into the plan with "The" read as "It" (asked). Flagged to him, his call: Phase 4 runs by round (his ruling of 7 Oct), so the sentence is not exact there; a capacity raise can end a competition mid-phase.
**His answers to the plan's six questions, about 7:09 PM, verbatim: *"1 - yes.  2-above.  3-fix.  4-all rules go out with welcome e-mail.  5-ok.  6-no"*** — (1) the label reads "Competition week"; (2) the definition line is his wording above; (3) the Outcomes e-mail line is corrected to cover any bid activity; (4) the welcome e-mail carries every rule, so the two competition lines go into both pages' welcome e-mail builders; (5) no label on the admin page; (6) no list of the affected live weeks wanted. Still open, asked again: the Phase-4 "or round" words, the returning-winner sentence. NOT a go to build — awaited (§92).
**About 7:23 PM, verbatim: *"phase 4 - don't care about this wording, do what i said."*** — the definition line stays his sentence exactly ("…at any point this phase. It remains a competition week for the duration of the phase."), with no Phase-4 round wording; the returning-winner sentence is STILL OPEN: the same minute he wrote *"returning winner - be more clear"* — explained again with an example, his yes / no awaited. His go to build: asked, awaited (§92).
**RULED about 7:24 PM, verbatim: *"Dr. A must come back with the same rules as anyone else"*** — given on Claude's example of a winner who removes a 5 from a competition week and wants to return when the entry level is 3. **This WITHDRAWS the returning-winner exception he ruled the same day about 11:33 AM (§249: *"returning winner should be same bid only or stronger"*), live in staff 195:** someone who leaves a competition week comes back under the entry level like any newcomer, whether or not they were winning when they left. Told to him: it reverses the morning's ruling; the Remove box must then warn a winner too; it joins the 196 plan (PLAN-196 §6a). NOT a go to build (§92).
**HIS GO, about 7:28 PM, verbatim: *"tonights rule.  go."*** — given after he asked *"does that contradict what i said before?"* and was shown the morning's ruling beside tonight's: the returning-winner exception is withdrawn, and the build (staff 196 / admin 358, all parts of PLAN-196) is approved. Built outside the folder he pushes from (START-HERE rule 4).
**AS BUILT — STAFF 196 / ADMIN 358, LIVE `37d200c`: he pushed 7 Oct 2026 about 9:45 PM (verbatim *"pushed."*), served twice, the pushed pages md5-identical to the gated copies** (gated copies staff `aee2a457a16e04e5445dc30f63828611`, admin `91b02a81000100967ece89c51d2d4e8e`; record: the `vacation-kp.github.io/BUILD-LOG.md` row; audits: `tests/docs/REVIEW-196-2026-10-07.md`). (0) `_wwContested` replays the phase's (in Phase 4 the round's) change log: a competition = at one moment the people with a bid on the week, plus FTE won there in an earlier phase, came to more than it has available. Where the record is thin it errs strict — a log entry is written after its bid and a failed write is ignored by the page, so the floors fill gaps, an unrecorded return is inferred, an entry with no usable time (or one more than ten minutes ahead of the reader's clock) counts throughout, and a log with no reset stamp (the admin's "Clear Change Log" writes none) counts every floor (195's answer). It never says "competition" where 195 said "not" (fuzzed). (0b) No returning-winner exception: the Remove box warns a winner too; the removal records no win. (A) "Competition week" at the top right of the card and in the bid box's week panel, only on a competition week someone still has a bid on. (B) 12 hours on both pages, proportions exact. (C) His definition line, the rule line, the Outcomes line, both welcome e-mails. **Three blind audits; two rounds of fixes inside `_wwContested` only** (a lost log write could hide a real competition; a junk name could throw; a cleared log; a clock running ahead; an ordering slip that was stricter than 195). **PUT TO HIM AT FILING, HIS CALL, not built (§92):** **F1 (HIGH)** — the change log can be appended to by any registered user with any time on it; under "at the same time" two forged entries naming the person who stayed (a back-dated removal and a later re-add, one write) can make a real fight read as "never together", so that person could then weaken their bid; in 195 an extra entry could only add people. No single forged entry does it any more. It shows in the admin Change Log only as an ordinary removal and re-add. Closing it needs a `firestore.rules` change (one document per entry, create-only, the writer's own initials, the server's time) — a rules build, not this one. **Residual, told:** two or more of ONE person's own log writes lost around the overlap can still hide a competition (measured 0 at 1–5% loss per write); a device clock that is slow, or less than ten minutes fast, can still shift an entry — never stricter than 195. **Told:** the admin's "Clear Change Log" mid-phase makes every floor count for the rest of the phase (195's stricter answer) until the next phase close.

## §255 — STAFF 198 / ADMIN 360: THE REMEMBERED LEVEL IS THE LAST PERSON'S BID; A RETURNING BIDDER IS HELD TO IT LIKE ANYONE; ONE PUSH — 8 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 8 Oct 2026 V1". His lines verbatim.** 2:00 PM, on the opening report and its offer to explore: *"go."* — the exploration (read-only) and ONE plan, `tests/docs/PLAN-198-360-REMEMBERED-LEVEL-AND-PHASE-CLOSE-RESET-2026-10-08.md`, put to him with three questions. 2:19 PM: **"1-agree.  2-yes. 3-both.  go.  i may have to turn off the mac if it takes a while."**
**So, RULED:** (1) on an emptied competition week a RETURNING bidder is held to the remembered level like anyone else — not to their own earlier bid (a weaker-than-before bid costs a lowering as usual). (2) The three wordings as proposed: the rule line's last sentence *"If everyone leaves the week, a new bid must be at least as strong as the last bid that was on it."* (page and both welcome e-mails); the refusal *"This week has had a bidding competition. A new bid must be at least as strong as the last bid that was on it."*; the Remove box for the last person *"⚠️ This week has had a bidding competition. If you remove this bid, a new bid here, from you or anyone else, must be at least as strong as this bid."* (3) *"both"* — read as: both parts in ONE push (staff 198 + admin 360). **The go to build is given.**
**As planned and told to him before the go:** the remembered level = the bid the last person had when they removed it, read from the change log (no new document, no rules change, no extra reads; a thin record errs strict); the "Competition week" label stays on an emptied competition week; winners at 1 and 5 — the 5 leaves, then the 1 → the week needs a bid as strong as the 1 for the rest of the phase; it binds on push day for weeks already empty; admin: Begin Phase refuses while a change-log reset is owed (at the door and, on the server's word, at the moment of action; no override), Complete Phase resets in the retry's order (§254's 1:44 PM rulings). Reverses §249 point 2 as §254 ordered.

## §256 — AFTER THE AUDITS OF 198 / 360: TWO PEOPLE MAKE A COMPETITION; A RETURNER'S WEAKER RE-BID IS NOT "NEVER LEFT"; AN EMERGENCY "BEGIN ANYWAY" — 8 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 8 Oct 2026 V1". Staff 198 / admin 360 were built and audited in the cloud that afternoon (three blind lanes, a red team, a verifier: safe to deploy; `tests/docs/REVIEW-198-360-2026-10-08.md`), NOT filed, NOT pushed. Three items were put to him as HIS CALL; his answers, verbatim.**
**6:25 PM:** *"1-yes. 2-this is a great catch, thank you. In phase 3 and four, there will be many weeks with only one FTE available. I think at least two, possibly three are actually needed to make it a competition week. two is probably more realistic unless you have a better idea? 3-if the emergency override is easy, safe, and effective, we should do it. Why wouldn't I want it?"* — (1) the follow-up fix for the false "competition" (a returner's weaker re-bid, logged "lowered", read by the replay as "never left") is wanted; (2) one person bigger than the room left is NOT a competition — at least two people; (3) an override wanted if easy, safe and effective. Claude's answers: two, not three (three would miss the commonest real fight, two people on a one-FTE week); the override's one downside is that it is a way round the safeguard, so it is offered only after the retry has really failed; ONE plan of three additions (A two people · B the returner · C the override) with three questions.
**6:48 PM:** **"1-leave definition same for now.  file to look at it again another time.  2-begin. 3- go"** — (1) the definition SENTENCE in Rules & Reminders stays as it is (Claude's reading, said to him at once: the two-person rule itself goes in, the wording is revisited another time — FILED in TODO); (2) the override's shape is **"begin"**: begin the next phase anyway and leave the old log (Claude had recommended "reset without archiving, behind a forced backup"; his choice stands — the consequence, told to him before he chose: the staff page goes on counting the finished phase's competitions for the whole new phase); (3) the go to build all three into the unpushed 198 / 360, with a fresh independent audit of A and B (they change the definition).
**So, RULED:** (A) a bidding competition needs at least TWO people with a bid on the week at the same moment who together (with whoever holds it from an earlier phase) come to more than it has available — amends §251; the label, the entry level, the no-weakening rule and the remembered level all follow. (B) In the replay, a "lowered" entry by someone the record has off the week is an ARRIVAL at that moment (the page logs a weaker re-bid that way), not proof they never left. (C) Admin: when "↻ Retry log reset" has FAILED on this page and a reset is still owed, the refusal dialog offers "begin Phase N anyway" behind a second, red confirmation; Begin Phase then goes through with the old log left as it is.
**As built and FILED (8 Oct 2026, about 7:40 PM; NOT pushed):** staff 198 `cf2d05cb200abba80940607ef277c0bd`, admin 360 `42e1299841ab5ce71709c6da124eaae0`; the record is the `vacation-kp.github.io/BUILD-LOG.md` row and `tests/docs/REVIEW-198-360-2026-10-08.md`. (A) and (B) are two edits inside `_wwContested`; nothing else in the staff page moved for them. (C) as ruled, with three tightenings the audit asked for: the "a retry has failed" memory is of THAT PHASE only; a retry that works, or a Begin Phase that finds nothing owed, drops an armed confirmation; the confirmation is used up, and its message shown, only once the phase has really begun. Audited again (a blind lane with a red team on the definition: 743,348 states of honest play, false competition 0, missed 0; a blind lane on the way out; a second verifier on its fixes): SAFE TO DEPLOY — yes. **Told to him:** on push day any week labelled a competition week only because of one oversized bidder, or only because of a returner, loses the label and its limits; the trade-off of (B) (a person whose arrival entry is lost or mis-stamped, who later lowers by Edit beside a third bidder, makes the week stop reading as a competition week); the way-out dialog over-warns for Phase 3 → 4.

Go. I really like this artifact test you did.  Add that as an idea to consider more often when building.  Very helpful."* — recorded as START-HERE rule 19. ## §259 — THE BID BOX'S CHOICES BECOME BUTTONS ON THE PAGE (NOT THE PHONE'S DROP-DOWN): "A IS GREAT"; CONTINUE STAYS ON SCREEN; THE SWAP STEP THE SAME; "SWAP BIDS" LARGER — 10 Oct 2026

**Raised by him, 10 Oct 2026 about 8:48 AM (session "Vacation Auction 10 Oct 2026 V1"), on the live staff 201, verbatim:** *"I still think the bid drop down could be better. What do professional sites do for this without building a separate mobile page?  The problem now is that usable bids appear too high sometimes and also block the new message we have about comp weeks. I want the best solution you have to see the message and make the usable bids more visible and the unusable bids visible, but out of the way. Think hard."* A minute later: *"Away from Mac for a while"*.
**Claude's answer, as read in live staff 201:** the bid box is the phone's own drop-down (`<select id="prioritySelect">`, thirteen choices); a phone draws that list itself — where it opens, what it scrolls to and what it covers are not the page's to decide, which is why the usable bids land too high and the list sits over the competition note. For a list this short, sites lay the choices out as buttons on the page (shoe sizes, time slots), the unavailable ones greyed; the same buttons serve a desktop and a phone, so no separate mobile page. Recommended: buttons, with the drop-down kept in the page out of sight as the one thing the bid is read from, so every rule and every check runs as it does now. Costs told to him: a taller box; the 200 / 201 suites and the sweep to revise; the "Not available" heading inside the drop-down (201, that morning) no longer seen. A published phone test page (rule 19) showed three versions on three example weeks: the live drop-down, **A** — usable numbers as big buttons, the rest in one small grey "Not available" row underneath — and **B** — every number in its usual place, the unusable ones greyed where they stand.
**RULED, 10 Oct 2026 about 9:55 AM, verbatim: *"A is great. Would be nice for continue to appear. Swap should get the same treatment and ideally “swap bids” would appear with larger text. We are now in P3 and will push during."*** — version A; Cancel / Continue to be on screen without scrolling; the Swap step's list of weeks as buttons too; "⇄ Swap bids" larger; **Phase 3 is running** and the build goes out during it.
**The plan put to him (staff 202 / admin 363), with a second phone test page — the button version alone, opening full-screen as the site does, the Swap step included:** (1) the buttons and the small "Not available" row; (2) Cancel / Continue stuck to the bottom of the box, proposed for all four steps (Select, Confirm, Swap, Confirm swap); (3) the Swap step's weeks as one full-width button each, the bid on the right; (4) "Swap bids" larger and MOVED UP to sit directly under the buttons with its question in small print beneath — where it sat it would be the thing pushed off screen in a full box, so the "Not available" row scrolls instead; admin: the one help sentence "under “Not available” in the dropdown" → "in the bid box". Not to change: the two drop-downs stay and stay what the bid is read from; every rule that decides which numbers are available; the review, confirm and swap checks; no rules file, no e-mails. Safety for a push during a live phase: if the buttons cannot be drawn the drop-down shows exactly as it is live now; a button exists only for a number the drop-down has marked usable and a tap checks that again.
**His answer, about 10:09 AM, verbatim: *"This is so much better, fantastic work."*** — taken as yes to the look (including "Swap bids" above "Not available"), NOT as a go (§92); the two open points were asked again.
**HIS GO, 10 Oct 2026 about 10:10 AM, verbatim: *"1-all. 2-go. Mac not available."*** — the button row sticks in all four steps; build. His Mac being off, it is built in the cloud and delivered as a zip (the §200 precedent); the push is his.
**AS BUILT — staff 202 / admin 363, in the cloud, 10 Oct 2026 (his Mac off; NOT yet in his folders, NOT pushed).** As planned, by `tests/apply202.py` from the pushed pair. Display only: six lines of 201 are removed or changed (the build number, three lines of "Swap bids" markup, the last line of each of `openPriorityModal` and `openSwapStep`); the rest is additions. **What the two blind reviews and the two verifiers made it gain, none of it asked for by him, all of it display (record: `tests/docs/REVIEW-202-363-2026-10-10.md`):** (a) a tap in the first 350 ms after the buttons appear, or after a press on Continue / ← Edit / ← Back, chooses nothing — without it a double tap on a week card silently chose the strongest number, and a double tap on "← Edit" changed the number already chosen; (b) "Please select a bid", pressed for from further down a box that now scrolls, brings the box back to its top; (c) on a phone held sideways the button row does not stay put (it covered the choices) — it ends the box as before; (d) what scrolls behind the button row fades out above it, the sign that there is more below; (e) the box starts at its top on every opening; the keyboard goes inside it; Enter right after opening chooses nothing; a button reached with Tab is never under the row; (f) on a wide screen the box is kept inside the window; (g) the grey of the "Not available" row darkened to the contrast of ordinary text. **Wordings that are Claude's, shown to him on the test page, not expressly approved:** *"Tap a number to choose your bid."* · *"Your bid: N"* · the buttons read *"NP"* where the drop-down read "NP (No Priority)". **Put to him at the hand-over, not blocking:** on the smallest phones (an iPhone SE or 8 with Safari's bars showing) the fullest box still opens with "Swap bids" one short scroll below the fold — it is on screen on 390×664 and larger.
**HIS ANSWER to the three points put to him at the hand-over, 10 Oct 2026 about 3:29 PM, verbatim: *"I agree with you on all of the calls."*** — all three stand AS BUILT: (1) on the smallest phones the fullest box opens with "Swap bids" one short scroll down; (2) the buttons read "NP", not "NP (No Priority)"; (3) the two hint wordings *"Tap a number to choose your bid."* and *"Your bid: N"* are approved. Nothing in the build changes; staff 202 `e97198c5…` / admin 363 `632abc2b…` remain the gated copies. Still NOT filed (his Mac off) and NOT pushed.
**FILED about 4:00 PM, 10 Oct 2026 (his *"back"*, 3:54 PM): the Mac's bases matched the zip's manifest, 20 files unpacked with `unzip -p` md5-identical, Mac gates green (inline scripts 4/4 both pages, battery 127 suites / 5,507 assertions exit 0, isolation 36/36, the new suite 80/80 and FAILED 52 of 55 on `f6a3761`), `status.mjs` clean.**

## §260 — A HISTORY OF EACH USER'S VISITS ON THE ADMIN SITE (NOT ONLY THE LAST SIGN-IN); AND HIS IDEA OF A "BLINDED" (ANONYMOUS) BIDDING SWITCH — 10 Oct 2026

**HIS ASK, 10 Oct 2026 (V2), 4:12 PM, verbatim:** *"I'm interested in saving multiple logins for each user to be visible on the admin site. It would help in terms of knowing when users accessed the site. I'm unsure of how to determine when the site was accessed by each user because frequently logging in isn't necessary if the browser was just minimized. It's helpful in case users complain they didn't have time to update bids, but turns out that they had been on the site and just chosen not to."*
**As read in live staff 202 / admin 363 (read-only):** `vacations/loginLog` holds ONE record per user (`{at,phase,round}`), overwritten at each sign-in by `_recordLogin`, called once at the end of `completeSignIn`; a page left open, minimized or brought back writes nothing. The rules confine a registered user to their OWN initials key in that document (`writesOnlyOwnKeys`); only the admin reads it. User Activity (`_activityRows`, `renderUserActivity`) shows the last record.
**The plan put to him (one turn):** a visit recorded when the page is used, one entry per quiet gap; kept INSIDE the user's own key (no rules change, no new document, no new listener), never awaited; User Activity shows "Last active", a visit count for the phase and a per-person list; the admin page trims to the newest 300 per person; Clear records and Reset Auction wipe it as today. Limits told to him: it shows the page was used under their sign-in, not what was looked at; it starts from the push; a user could alter their OWN list with technical tricks (closing that needs a rules change — advised against).
**HIS ANSWERS, 4:26 PM, verbatim:** *"1 - can you know if the page is just viewed on phone/screen? If so, that's perfect. 2- 30 min gap good. 3-good. 4. will push during P3."* — RULED: the 30-minute gap; the display as proposed (inside User Activity); the push during live Phase 3. On (1) he prefers "viewed on the phone / screen" if the page can know it — Claude's answer the same turn: the browser reports when the page is ON SCREEN (in front, not minimized, not a background tab, the phone not locked or on another app) but not whether anyone is looking; proposed: an entry each time the page comes onto the screen (opened, or brought back), at most one per 30 minutes, marked when the person also touched it. **His go to BUILD: asked for, not yet given (§92).**
**HIS SECOND IDEA, the same message, verbatim:** *"I'm also interested in exploring the idea of a new section in admin controls that can make each user's page blinded to other users. This would allow for doing an anonymous bidding process. IT could be part of this build or the next. I would want to be able to toggle it on/off. It would make it so that each user can only see their own bids and all others would be generic in a clean, easy to visualize way. Having the FTE visible could be somewhat problemsatic since people might be able to determine who is who based on FTE total. That doesn't matter so much, but if you have a solution, I'm interested. There are pluses and minuses to a blinded process related to bullying/intimidation/collusion/helpfullness/friendship that can tip in either direction."*
**As read (a first look, not the full exploration):** the staff week card lists every bid on the week as initials · bid · outcome letter (the "Bids:" list in `renderCalendar`), with the week's FTE as TOTALS only (Available / Winning / Remaining / under review / Losing) — no per-person FTE is printed; the two "changes" lists (`renderNotifications`) name who made each change; the bids document (`vacations/schedule`) is readable by every signed-in account and carries everyone's initials. **Told to him:** (a) hiding the names on the page is a display change; the names would still arrive in every browser, so it stops casual looking, not a determined colleague — a blind nobody can see through needs the data and the rules reshaped, which is large and not for a live auction; (b) every place that names a colleague must be found first (cards, both changes lists, the phase history, the e-mails) — one missed place breaks the blind; (c) advised as its OWN build after the visit history, not the same one, and switched only between auctions or phases, never mid-phase. **NOT explored in full, NOT planned, NOT approved, nothing built (§92).**
**HIS GO AND HIS ANSWERS, 10 Oct 2026, 4:32 PM, verbatim:** *"1- God. 2-yes. 3-users should still those bid #s and w/r/l. blinding option can be next build."* then *"1- Go, not god"*. — (1) **GO on the visit history** as described: an entry each time the page comes onto the screen, at most one per 30 minutes, marked when the person also touched it; inside User Activity; trimmed by the admin page; pushed during live Phase 3. To be built as staff 203 / admin 364, no rules change. (2) Blinding: hidden on the page, though not from a determined technical colleague, is good enough. (3) In blinded mode users still see each anonymous bid's number and W / R / L. (4) Blinding is the NEXT build — explore, ONE plan, his go (§92); nothing planned or built.
**BUILT, REVIEWED AND FILED the same evening as staff 203 / admin 364 (about 5:40 PM; gated copies staff `a296fd785a4b263d8557e3529e1dc134`, admin `fff33be38a3e515c7b813b199f23bf37`; NOT pushed — the push is his).** As built: a visit is noted when the signed-in board comes onto the screen (sign-in finished, the tab or window brought back or brought to the front, the phone switched back to it) or is touched; at most one entry per 30 minutes per browser; marked when the person tapped, clicked, typed or turned the wheel during it; kept inside the user's own key of `vacations/loginLog` as ONE short code per entry; never awaited, never able to delay a bid. User Activity: "Last active", a "N visits this phase" button per row that opens the person's list, "On the site during Phase N" in the summary. **Two things differ from what he was told at 4:12–4:26 PM, both found by the independent review and told to him at filing:** (1) each person's list keeps the newest **250**, not 300 — a Firestore document may hold 40,000 index entries, the whole roster shares this one, and at 300 with the first design it would have filled and silently stopped recording sign-ins as well as visits; (2) besides the page coming onto the screen, a window brought to the FRONT also counts as a view. Limits, as told: on screen is not the same as looked at; times are the user's own device clock; the record is written by the user's own browser, so it is good evidence rather than proof; recording starts at the push. Record: BUILD-LOG row 203, `tests/docs/REVIEW-203-364-2026-10-10.md`.
**PUSHED BY HIM, 10 Oct 2026 about 5:49 PM, verbatim: *"pushed"* — all three repos (auction `b6b155b`, tests `d931096`, hub `56f392b`), during live Phase 3. Verified the same minute: served twice as staff 203 / admin 364; the pushed pages md5-identical to the gated copies `a296fd78…` / `fff33be3…`. STAFF 203 / ADMIN 364 ARE LIVE. Next auction honesty baseline `b6b155b`.**

## §261 — THE BLINDED-BIDDING SWITCH: HIS BRIEF FOR THE EXPLORATION — 10 Oct 2026
**HIS WORDS, 10 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 10 Oct 2026 V3", 6:39 PM), verbatim, in answer to "Shall I start the exploration?":** *"Yes.  I want the general format to stay nearly identical, just every initials should be blinded or turned into a generic icon that's clean.  I want a toggle on the admin site in the controls section to change between blinded and un-blinded if that's possible without causing too much damage.  This would be a between phase deploy, once P3 is done."*
**RULED by this:** (1) GO on the read-only exploration — NOT a go to build (§92). (2) The staff page keeps its layout "nearly identical"; every colleague's initials are blinded or replaced by one clean generic mark. (3) The switch is a toggle in the admin's Controls section, blinded / un-blinded — "if that's possible without causing too much damage". (4) The deploy is BETWEEN phases, after Phase 3 is done. Carried from §260: a page-level blind is good enough; anonymous bids keep their number and W / R / L.
**EXPLORED the same evening, read-only (staff 203 `a296fd78…`, admin 364 `fff33be3…`), two independent readings:** FOUR places on the staff page paint a colleague's initials (the "Bids:" list, "Other Bids:" on locked weeks, both changes lists) and no other; no e-mail from either page names a colleague; no per-person FTE is printed; the switch is one new value in `adminSettings`, no rules change. ONE plan: `tests/docs/PLAN-204-365-BLINDED-BIDDING-2026-10-10.md`; a phone test page of three looks published for him. Put to him with five questions.
**HIS ANSWERS to the five questions, 10 Oct 2026, 8:04 PM, verbatim:** *"1-A. 2-agree.  3-move it to rules/reminders.  it should either say Bidder names are hidden for this auction (or are displayed or shown or other?  what would you say?). 4-agree.  5-good."* — RULED: (1) the mark is A, the small grey person mark; (2) one confirmation each way on the switch, and a second red box when un-blinding while bidding is open; (3) the notice goes in Rules & Reminders, NOT under the board title — its wording asked of Claude, not yet settled; (4) FTE totals stay as they are; (5) the card's wording as proposed ("Blinded bidding" · "Hide bidder names on the staff site" · the description). **Not a go to build.**
**ON THE NOTICE'S WORDING, 10 Oct 2026, 8:09 PM, verbatim** (Claude had proposed "Bidder names are currently hidden. You see your own bids as usual; every other bid shows without initials.", shown only while blinded, and asked whether it stays out of the welcome e-mail because the switch can be flipped): *"1-wording good, but rephrase for auction.  2-blinding would almost certainly be for the entire auction, wouldn't go back and forth."* — RULED: the line says "for this auction", not "currently"; blinding is meant for a whole auction, not switched back and forth. Re-worded and put back to him: "Bidder names are hidden for this auction. You see your own bids as usual; every other bid shows without initials." Whether the welcome e-mail carries the line while the switch is on: asked again, not yet answered. **Not a go to build.**
**HIS GO, 10 Oct 2026, 8:10 PM, verbatim** (asked: 1 should the welcome e-mail carry the line while the switch is on; 2 go to build staff 204 / admin 365, built and gated in the cloud, the push his for after Phase 3): *"1-yes. 2-go."* — RULED: the welcome e-mail carries the line while the switch is on; **GO to build staff 204 / admin 365** as planned and as ruled above (mark A; a confirmation each way and a second red box when un-blinding while bidding is open; the line in Rules & Reminders only while blinded, "Bidder names are hidden for this auction. You see your own bids as usual; every other bid shows without initials."; FTE totals as they are; the card's wording). Deployed BETWEEN phases, after Phase 3 — the push is his.
**OWNER-FOUND while the build was in its gates, 10 Oct 2026, 9:01 PM, verbatim, with a phone screenshot of his own row ("AB 1/2/3 W [✎]", the ✕ alone on a second line):** *"This woud be a problem.  It must size right for a phone and not have the cancel wrap to a 2nd line."* Then, 9:04 PM: *"will check in tomorrow.  mac should stay on."* — Measured the same hour on the LIVE 203: the wrap is there today, at every two-to-a-row phone width up to 393px with a three-number bid (it is not something blinding causes). Taken as his decision to fix it (§92; he was away, so the reading is stated here): built INTO 204 as its own edit — the row with ✎ and ✕ keeps them on the bid's line wherever each can be at least 20px wide (every two-letter case measured, 381–1280px), and takes a second line at full size only where it cannot (very large text; wide three-letter initials with a three-number bid on the narrowest phones) — never a button too thin to press. Desktop unchanged.
**BUILT, REVIEWED AND FILED 10 Oct 2026 about 10:35 PM as staff 204 / admin 365 (gated copies staff `c44cc16c412109b01a9e04ef045c048b`, admin `f20a92444726529ef8bd68d41f46647f`; NOT pushed — the push is his, AFTER Phase 3, his ruling).** As built: his rulings above, word for word (mark A; the card's wording; one confirmation each way and a second red one when un-blinding while bidding is open; the line "Bidder names are hidden for this auction. You see your own bids as usual; every other bid shows without initials." in Rules & Reminders — placed after the bid rules, before the strategy and conduct lines — and in the welcome e-mail, both only while the switch is on; FTE totals as they are). **Differs from what he was told, or was not put to him — told at filing:** (1) the second red box also appears when the admin page has not yet loaded the auction state (it cannot tell whether bidding is open); (2) the own-row fix above rides in this build; (3) the admin's ON box says the welcome e-mail gains the line. **Left for him, NOT built:** (a) a Full Restore writes the settings as the backup holds them, so an older backup un-blinds without any box (and a blinded backup re-blinds) — the restore path was not touched; (b) a tab asleep or offline when the switch goes ON may paint new bids with initials for a moment on waking; (c) Reset Auction leaves the switch as it is; (d) the mark and the own row have not been seen on a real iPhone. Record: BUILD-LOG row 204, `tests/docs/REVIEW-204-365-2026-10-10.md`.
**PUSHED BY HIM, 11 Oct 2026 about 7:07 AM, verbatim: *"pushed all 3, decided it was ok.  handoff."* — all three repos (auction `a0fb452`, tests `55f470f`, hub `d8fd9ed`), during live Phase 3: HIS DECISION, replacing his 10 Oct ruling that it would wait for the end of Phase 3. Verified the same minute: served twice as staff 204 / admin 365; the pushed pages md5-identical to the gated copies `c44cc16c…` / `f20a9244…`. STAFF 204 / ADMIN 365 ARE LIVE; the switch is OFF until he turns it on. Next auction honesty baseline `a0fb452`. Still unanswered, his calls: whether a Full Restore should leave the switch as it is (§261 "Left for him" (a)).**
**HIS ANSWER after the hand-over, 11 Oct 2026, 7:10 AM, verbatim:** *"looks good.  full restore should remember the switch as it was during restore.  reset auction as well."* — RULED: (1) a Full Restore keeps the Blinded bidding switch as it stands at the moment of the restore, whatever the backup holds (Claude's reading of "as it was during restore", put back to him to confirm); (2) Reset Auction likewise leaves it as it stands — which is what the live 365 already does (it merge-writes one other key). (1) needs a small admin build (366): NOT planned in full, NOT approved to build, nothing built (§92).
**CORRECTED BY HIM, 11 Oct 2026, 7:12 AM, verbatim** (Claude had read the 7:10 ruling as "keep the switch as it stands at the moment of the restore" and asked him to confirm): *"sorry, keep as the backup had it"* — RULED: a Full Restore sets the Blinded bidding switch to what the BACKUP holds. That is exactly what the live 365 does (the backup's settings are written as they are), so NO BUILD: admin 366 is not needed and the reading above is withdrawn. Reset Auction leaves the switch as it stands, also as the live 365 does. Offered to him and not answered: a line in the restore's confirmation box when the backup's switch differs from the live one (today a restore changes it without saying so).
**Status:** LIVE since 11 Oct 2026; nothing to build.

## §262 — TWO IDEAS FILED FOR EXPLORATION IN A LATER SESSION: "VACATION BUCKS" IN PLACE OF BID NUMBERS (FUTURE AUCTIONS ONLY); AND NO LEAVING A WEEK FROM A WINNING POSITION — 10 Oct 2026
**HIS WORDS, 10 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 10 Oct 2026 V3", 8:17 PM, sent while staff 204 / admin 365 was being built), verbatim:** *"For when this is done and for a different session, I want to explore the possibility of changing from Bid numbers to what I would call Vacation Bucks.  The idea would be that users would be allocated a certain amount of vacation bucks instead of bid numbers.  User could spend their vacation bucks as they desire.  Bucks could be contributed, but only withdrawn from a week if they move from winning to either under review or losing.  This is a fundamental change to how the auction would work and would only be for future auctions, not this one.  It would probably provide a greater degree of freedom for user and would more closely resemble a real auction.  I have no idea if this is something where the admin site could control whether a specific auction would use bid numbers vs. vacation bucks, but that would be ideal.   most rules would stay the same.  the biggest difference is that users couldn't leave a week with their bucks from a winning position.  I am actually considering adding this rule to the numbered bids option as well and would like that idea filed for exploration.  There is a problem when a user bids up a week and is winning and then changes their mind and leaves.  A thought I had was that perhaps a user could only leave from a competition week if that bid was needed on another week where they went from winning to review/losing or review to losing."*
**Filed as he asked — two ideas, both for exploration in a DIFFERENT session, after the blinded-bidding build is done:** (1) VACATION BUCKS — each user is allocated an amount of bucks instead of bid numbers and spends them as they wish; bucks can be added to a week, and taken back only when the bid moves from winning to Under Review or losing; most rules unchanged; FUTURE auctions only, never this one; ideally an admin setting chooses, per auction, bid numbers or bucks. (2) THE SAME RULE FOR NUMBERED BIDS — a user could not leave a week from a winning position; his thought: leaving a COMPETITION week allowed only when that bid is needed on another week where the user went from winning to review / losing, or from review to losing. The second is the idea he filed on 8 Oct 2026 (§253, the limit on cancelling a winning bid on a competition week), now widened by "review to losing".
**Status:** filed only — NOT explored, NOT planned, NOT approved, nothing built (§92).
**11 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 11 Oct 2026 V1"), idea (1) only.** 7:15 AM, his words: *"the possibility of vacation bucks"*. Claude gave a first reading from §262 and a count of where bid numbers appear in the live pages (staff 204 / admin 365) — no plan, nothing built. 7:19 AM, HIS ORDER, verbatim: *"file this as a future item todo"*. **So: Vacation Bucks is a FUTURE ITEM in `TODO.md` §1 — not explored further, not planned, not approved; future auctions only, as he said on 10 Oct.** The first reading (Claude's view, none of it ruled): (i) if bucks cannot leave a winning bid, bids on a week only rise, so most of the competition-week rules (§245 – §258), bailing, the "disruptive bid" and harvesting fall away, and ties become rarer; (ii) "pay what you bid" invites raising one buck at a time — more changes, e-mails and timer resets — and the usual cure is that each winner on a week pays the lowest winning bid, the excess returned at phase close; (iii) advised AGAINST an admin switch inside the current pages (two engines in the same two files) and FOR a separate copy of the site with the numbered one left frozen; (iv) weeks of work, after this auction closes; (v) each number-based rule (summer 1–7, the high-demand floor, the 6-bid cap, NP, bid lowerings) needs a bucks translation; (vi) a budget rewards strategists more than "1 is strongest" does — his call. Three questions were put and are UNANSWERED: pay-your-bid or lowest-winning-bid; one budget for the whole auction; the same allocation for all or scaled by FTE.

## §263 — BAIL TRACKING TAKEN UP AGAIN: EXPLORE IT, AND EXPLORE A LOOK BACK THROUGH THIS AUCTION'S CHANGE LOG; HIS DEFINITION OF BAILING — 11 Oct 2026
**HIS WORDS, 11 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 11 Oct 2026 V1", 7:23 AM), verbatim:** *"explore bail tracking.  also explore how you could look back at the change log to find incidents that happened this auction.  bailing is when a user bids up a week and then bails from a winning position.  it's arguably the worst behavior we can do in an auction like this."*
**What this does:** it lifts the "wait" of §253 (1) for EXPLORATION only — read-only, no plan approved, nothing built (§92). **His definition, now on the record: bailing = a user bids up a week and then leaves it from a WINNING position.** (On 6 Oct he also said *"Bailing at all needs to be tracked"* with a narrative of winning / losing / review — how the two sit together was put to him.)
**Explored the same turn, read-only, live admin 365 / staff 204 — what was found:** (1) the admin's Fair Play Monitor ALREADY flags a "Winning-bid cancel" (`_fpAnalyzePhase`, `winCancel`): a removal from a winning position that changed another bidder's projection, with a count of the other users who raised on that week while the bid was live; it covers every phase (archive + live log). (2) What it does not do against his definition: it never asks whether the leaver bid the week UP (their own raises, or the week being a competition week); it needs the recorded "affected someone" fact, which exists only on entries written by staff 183 or later (1 Oct 2026), so the first week of Phase 1 can carry no such flag; its winning test replays the phase's log from an empty board (whether weeks held from an earlier phase are counted there is TO BE CONFIRMED in code before any build); it gives no account of how long the bid stayed, whether the bid number was used elsewhere afterwards and how soon, or who gained. (3) The change log holds what a look back needs — per entry: user, week, action (added / edited / lowered / removed), the bid value, the time; `vacations/changesArchive` holds each finished phase, `vacations/changes` the current one; both are in his backup file. (4) Production cannot be read from a session: the look back runs on a backup JSON he attaches, by a read-only script in the cloud — no build, no deploy, nothing written to the site. The result names colleagues, so it goes to him in the chat only and is NEVER committed (these repos are public).
**Put to him, unanswered:** what "bids up a week" is measured by (the week being a competition week when they left; or their own raises; or both); whether a leave from Under Review or losing is listed too, apart from the bails; whether to run the look back first on a backup before anything is built (advised: yes).
**Status:** explored only — NOT planned, NOT approved, nothing built (§92).
**HIS ANSWERS, 11 Oct 2026, 7:27 AM, verbatim: *"1-good.  2-agree. 3-attached."*** — (1) "bids up a week" is measured by the site's own test: the week was a competition week when they left (their own raises are told in the story, not required); (2) only a leave from a WINNING position is a bail; leaves from Under Review or losing go in a quieter second list; (3) he attached a backup (exported 11 Oct 7:27 AM, admin 365) for the look back.
**THE LOOK BACK, RUN THE SAME TURN (read-only; script `tests/bail-lookback.py`, which holds no names; the result went to him in the chat only and is not in any repo).** How it works: each phase's log is replayed in time order (Phase 1 and 2 from the archive, Phase 3 from the live log, entries dated before the phase began left out); the position at each removal is judged by the engine's own steps (`computeApprovals`: groups by score, the FP-safe fit, the review threshold, earlier-phase winners holding their room); a week is a competition week from the first moment two or more current-phase bidders plus the earlier-phase winners exceed its capacity. **The replay was checked against the site's own record: at the end of each phase it reproduces every bid the site holds (26 / 155 / 54, none missing, none extra, none different), and it agrees with the stored projections on 48 of 49 weeks (one Under Review on one week differs).** Capacity: each phase's own completed record; Phase 3 uses the Phase 2 record (the backup carries no current capacity table). FTE: today's.
**What it found, by shape (no names here — public repo):** 95 removals by users across Phases 1–3. **7 bails** (a winning bid taken off a competition week, no bid put back on that week within the hour) — all in Phase 2, between 5 and 8 Oct, by six people; in five the bid number went to another week within a minute to 14 hours, in one it was not used again, in three nobody's projection improved because the week had room by then. **7 more** took a winning bid off a competition week and put a bid back on the same week within the hour — four of them came back one step weaker after moving their strongest number to another week (the pattern "⇄ Swap bids", staff 199, was built for). 56 left a competition week from Under Review or losing; 25 left a winning position on a week that never was a competition week. None in Phase 1 or Phase 3.
**Confirmed in code the same turn:** the Fair Play Monitor's winning test (`_fpWasWinning`) measures against the week's whole capacity and does NOT count the room held by earlier-phase winners — so in Phases 2–4 it can call a bid winning when it was not.
**HIS RULING ON WHAT A BAIL IS, 11 Oct 2026, 10:59 AM, verbatim:** *"I think it's currently fair game to bail on a week when that bid is needed elsewhere.  It's when the bid is not needed that creates trouble.  This implies that the user went from winning to review/losing on a different week and then had to use this winning bid from the week they are bailing on.  This is what I want to track.  Winning bid cancels in and of themselves cannot be considered bad behavior when a bid requires harvesting, so I think this should be re-worked to look for a more narrow term I'm calling bailing or maybe you have a better term for it.  Timer stalling can still be useful to monitor."*
**So (this narrows the 7:23 AM definition above):** taking a winning bid off a week is FAIR when its number is needed elsewhere — the user's bid on a DIFFERENT week went from winning to Under Review or losing, and the number is then used there. A BAIL is a winning bid taken off when the number was NOT needed. The Fair Play Monitor's "Winning-bid cancel" flag is to be RE-WORKED into that narrow thing; timer stalling stays. No go to build (§92).
**The look back re-run with that test the same turn (`tests/bail-needed.py`, read-only; names to him in the chat only).** Of the 14 winning bids taken off a competition week: 6 pass his test (the number went, within a minute, to another week where the same user's bid had dropped from winning) — four of those also put a weaker bid back on the week they came off; 3 never left the week (a bid was back on it within minutes, and the number went nowhere it was needed); **5 are bails by his test** (four people): the number was never used (1), went to a week where the user was already winning (1), or went to a week the user had no bid on (3 — in one of these the user did have a displaced bid elsewhere and did not use the number there). **Put to him, unanswered:** whether a number moved to a NEW week counts as "not needed"; whether a drop the user caused themselves counts; which other Fair Play flags stay (winning-bid lowering, late-timer move, over-limit lowering); the name (advised: keep "Bail").
**HIS ANSWERS, 11 Oct 2026, 11:11 AM, verbatim:** *"1- this is an in-between situation, but the spirit is similar to bailing.  If the user was kicked out some 3rd week, it's possible that the new week is a replacement for that in some way.  It's complicated because everything depends on so many other factors.  What do you think?  Can we apply some test to determine this?  2-I'm not sure what to make of this. 3-keep late timer move.  what's over-limit lowering?  4-yes"* — so: the late-timer move STAYS; a plan for the build is wanted; (1) and (2) are open and he asked for Claude's view and a test.
**Claude's answer, from a third run on the backup:** a loose replacement test (knocked out of winning anywhere in the last 48 h) clears all 14 cases — nearly everyone has been, in a busy phase — so it decides nothing; a tight one discriminates: the number went to a week the person had been knocked out of winning on, still there or not. With that and a 1-hour minimum stay, 2 of the 14 are bails. **Claude changed its position, 11 Oct 11:11 AM, on evidence:** at 7:23 AM it advised that the week being a competition week was enough for "bid up"; a 4-minute stay met that, so a minimum stay is now advised.
**THE PLAN, ONE document: `tests/docs/PLAN-366-BAIL-ENTRY-2026-10-11.md` — admin page only; six open questions; NOT approved, nothing built (§92).**
**HIS OBJECTIONS AND RULINGS, 11 Oct 2026, 11:38 AM, verbatim:** *"under the tight test, why 48 hours?  what if it becomes the entire phase?  Had MA been knocked out of a different week?  DR is very clearly bailing.  The in-between could be used to prove that it's been determined the move was Fair Play.  I think it would be useful to track what some view as unfair and I would have the data to prove that it was considered fair and why.    why does the bid need to be there for 1 hour?  in a flurry of changes, things could happen quickly?  it's the behavior that's important, not the timing in this case."* **11:39 AM:** *"retire winning bid lowering.  i'm ok including the warning line."*
**So:** (1) no time windows — the knock-out may be anywhere earlier in the phase; (2) the 1-hour minimum stay is WITHDRAWN; (3) the fair cases are to be LISTED with the reason they were judged fair, beside the bails; (4) "Winning-bid lowering" is RETIRED; (5) the warning line in Rules & Reminders is IN the build (so it is admin 366 + staff 205, the staff change one line of text). Still no go to build (§92).
**Claude's revision, run on the backup the same turn:** the test has no clock at all — "needed" = the number's NEXT placement is on a week where the person had been knocked out of winning BEFORE leaving and was not winning when they left; "never left" = back on the week before any colleague changed a bid there (true of all seven returns). The knock-out must precede the leave: in one case the person left a winning week for a new one, was knocked out there hours later and only then used the number there. Result: 14 listed — 3 bails, 8 needed, 3 never left. His question answered from the log: the person he asked about HAD been knocked out on two other weeks earlier in the phase, had already won both back by raising, and the number went to neither. Plan revised in place (`tests/docs/PLAN-366-BAIL-ENTRY-2026-10-11.md`); four questions put.
**HIS ANSWERS, 11 Oct 2026, 11:45 AM, verbatim:** *"1-yes. 2-it's ok, but is this also potentially timer stalling? Is it likely a mistake the user made? 3-yes."* — **RULED: the test of the revised plan (bail / fair-needed / fair-never-left, no clocks) — YES; "never left" = back on the week before any colleague changed a bid there — OK; the warning line's wording — YES** (*"Bailing — leaving a competition week from a winning position when that bid number is not needed on another week — and repeated last-minute bid activity are tracked by admin. Please play fairly."*). His go to build: NOT yet given (question 4 unanswered).
**His two questions, answered from the log's recorded timer facts:** of the three "never left" cases, two did not restart the countdown at all; one restarted it twice with 11.9 of 12 hours already on it (a number moved between the person's own two weeks — what "⇄ Swap bids" now does in one save). Today's timer-stalling flag only sees place-then-cancel, not cancel-then-place-back. Advised, and added to the plan: no new flag — each "never left" entry states whether it restarted the countdown and how much time was on it; the late-timer flag already catches a round trip near the end that changed someone's projection. Whether one was a mistake cannot be told from the log; the entry gives the facts.
**11:48 AM, his question, verbatim:** *"i still don't get never-left.  they did leave, but came back?"* — answered: yes, they left and came straight back; the name was wrong. **11:52 AM, verbatim:** *"coming back weaker should be ok, it's just a lowering.  and now only allowed if still winning and not lower than lowest winning bid.  I prefer bail and return actually."* — **RULED: the class is NAMED "Bail and return"** (in place of Claude's "never left" / "left and came straight back"); coming back weaker is fine — it is a lowering, which the competition rule already limits. Read by Claude as the NAME of the fair class (listed with its reason, not counted as a bail) — said to him in the same turn so he can correct it.
**HIS GO, 11 Oct 2026, 11:53 AM, verbatim: *"go.  during p3."*** — admin 366 + staff 205 approved to build as planned (`tests/docs/PLAN-366-BAIL-ENTRY-2026-10-11.md`); he will push during live Phase 3. ## §264 — TWO HARD CONTEXT LINES: NO NEW BUILD ABOVE 550,000 TOKENS, EVERYTHING STOPS AT 650,000; NO SESSION IS EVER COMPACTED — 11 Oct 2026
**HIS WORDS, 11 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 11 Oct 2026 V2", 4:37 PM), verbatim:** *"I stopped that session because it was trying to compact.  I would actually like you to update handoffs so that future sessions including this one refuse to keep going when at risk of exceeding 700k tokens."*
**What happened:** session "Vacation Auction 11 Oct 2026 V1" filed staff 205 / admin 366 at a context of 728,351, took his go for admin 367 at 4:29 PM, and tried to compact; he stopped it. Admin 367 was left part-built in that session's cloud workspace, which no other session can reach.
**Put to him the same turn (4:38 PM):** a refusal AT 700k cannot work, because the close itself costs tokens and a build costs far more — so two lines: (1) no new build starts above a first line (questions, filing a ruling, a docs fix still allowed); (2) at a second line everything stops and the closing checklist runs. Suggested 550,000 and 650,000, said to be a judgement and not a measurement (the files record the context at six closes, 453k to 728k, but not what a build or a close costs). Asked: are the numbers right, and is the second line absolute or can he override it in the moment.
**RULED 4:39 PM, verbatim:** *"this sounds perfect.  i want it absolute because i never want a conversation compacted."*
**So:** ABOVE 550,000 no new build starts — the session reports the reading and refuses. AT 650,000 all work stops and the closing checklist runs; ABSOLUTE, no override by Claude or by him in the moment; an unfinished build is not rushed — its scratch state goes to the outputs column as a zip and `TODO.md` §1 records where it stands. It binds every session, the one it was ruled in included.
**What it replaces:** §106's "report the reading, never block on it" and the standing line that a session under 700k is never offered a fresh one. The reading is still reported every time; it now also blocks. The two numbers are his — not to be moved, softened or added to.
**Where it is written:** `START-HERE.md` §4 (the Context paragraph and step 5) and the head of `HANDOFF.md`'s closing checklist. Docs only; nothing the auction serves is touched.
**Status:** STANDING.

## §266 — LOCKED WEEKS STAY IN THEIR MONTH ON THE STAFF BOARD, GREYED; A WON WEEK STAYS GREEN — 12 Oct 2026

**His idea, 12 Oct 2026, 8:10 AM (session "Vacation Auction 12 Oct 2026 V1"), verbatim:** *"I'm interested in making it so that the locked weeks appear on the main bidding board, but are greyed out during P2/P3.  Users want to see all the weeks for reference.  The red will be irritating to look at, so I think changing to greyed out would be better.  I'm unsure about P1 when it's likely that the majority of weeks would be greyed out."*
**As read in live staff 205 (`renderCalendar`):** every locked week (a per-week lock or the Freeze) is taken out of its month and drawn in a red-tinted, 60%-faded "🔒 Locked Weeks" section at the bottom; that card shows week, dates, special label, My Bid and Other Bids (this phase's bids only, with the W / R / L badges), a red LOCKED badge, no FTE lines and no click handler. A week the user won in an EARLIER phase or round shows nothing of theirs there (their old bid is not a this-phase bid). Phase 1 = the 6 high-demand weeks open, 46 locked; Phases 2–3 = Smart Lock locks the full weeks; a phase close and the timer's expiry lock all 52; Phase 4 rounds open chosen months; the Freeze sets `globalLock`. Staff page only. A phone test page was published to him (greyed in place, text at full strength, dashed grey edge, grey "🔒 Locked" tag). **Claude's slip, told to him:** the test page drew ✅ marks where the live page draws small letter badges (green W, purple R, red L; a solid green ✓ only for an approved result).
**His answers, 12 Oct 2026, 1:20 PM, verbatim:** *"I want the green check marks on greyed out weeks to be less noticeable.  I actually think that perhaps the week a user has won previously should perhaps stay green to indicate it's won?  Would be useful to users as a reference and easy to see.  My bid as a win already has a won badge on it for previous phases.  Phase 1 can keep today's layout, although it's possible that phase 1 in the future may have more weeks.  How would we handle that?  phase 4 rounds should be just like the phases.  In all respects, each P4 round is its own independent phase.  When every week is locked going forward, I want them to stay in place and turn grey.  Grey look is good,."*
**Ruled:** (1) locked weeks stay in their month, greyed (the test page's grey look approved), in Phases 2 and 3 and in every Phase 4 round; (2) the outcome badges on a grey card are made less noticeable; (3) a week the user won in an earlier phase or round stays GREEN on the board, locked or not, as a reference; (4) Phase 1 keeps today's layout; (5) whenever every week is locked, the weeks stay in place and turn grey. **Open, put to him:** how a future Phase 1 with more open weeks is handled; whether a win turns green from the moment its results are sent or from the next phase's start; whether (5) also holds during Phase 1. Nothing planned, nothing built (§92).
**Claude's slip, told to him the same turn (1:2x PM):** the first test page drew ✅ where the live page draws letter boxes; version 2 used the real boxes (grey on grey cards) and drew a won week green with "🏆 Won in Phase 1 — Locked In". Options put for a future Phase 1: leave it until it happens (recommended), an admin switch, or an automatic rule (advised against).

## §267 — WHEN BIDDING CLOSES NOTHING MOVES: EVERY WEEK DRAWN LOCKED AT ZERO, WITH OR WITHOUT AN ADMIN PAGE, IN THE LAYOUT IT HAD — 12 Oct 2026

Session "Vacation Auction 12 Oct 2026 V2". Phase 3 of the live auction CLOSED 12 Oct 2026 (his word, 5:50 PM); Phase 4 opens next year; he is planning for next year's auction now.

**His question, 5:10 PM, verbatim:** *"Re: the last build, Will weeks stay ordered in their months when that option is selected at phase close?  Or does it follow the setting that admin sets?"* — answered from the live code: a phase close locks every week (`_lockEveryWeek`), and with every week locked the board stays in place whatever the switch says (§266 (5)).

**5:15 PM:** *"I now want the weeks to follow the same setting that controls where they appears while bidding is live.  Do you agree that makes more sense?  Disagree if you think otherwise."* — Claude disagreed: with the switch at the bottom and every week locked the whole board becomes one list of 52 with no month headings, and the board jumps at the expiry either way.

**5:16 PM, owner-found:** *"Also, admin being logged in at some point changed how the weeks appeared at phase end when all weeks locked."* · *"I don't love that"* — cause, read in the code: only an open admin page writes the week locks (`_autoCloseOnExpiry` → `_lockEveryWeek`; `locks` is admin-only in the rules); the staff board drew from that record alone, so between the zero and an admin sign-in the open weeks still drew open, and the admin's lock-all then moved the board.

**5:20 PM:** *"1- are you saying Weeks don't actually lock at timer expiry?  I think they do. 2-why not b?  That way the weeks don't shift around at timer expiry?"* — answered: bidding stops at zero for everyone (the rules, on Google's clock); the lock RECORD waits for an admin page; B moves the open weeks instead of the locked ones.

**5:42 PM:** *"1- yes, they should lock at timer expiry even if admin isn't logged in.  Agree?  What I want is consistency.  I don't want weeks jumping all over the place."* — Claude agreed on the goal and proposed the display route (the staff page draws a closed auction as locked) over staff writes to `locks` or server code.

**5:50 PM:** *"P3 now closed.  p4 doesn't open until next year.  I am planning for next year's auction now.  I want P1 included."* — four ways put: A today's rule, B follow the switch, C hold the layout at the close, D Phase 1's switch in place. **5:54 PM:** *"I like C as well.  You said they turn grey.  Would weeks with the user's own bids still say their projection and be that color?"* — yes (207's rule; grey only without an own bid). **5:57 PM:** *"Yes. Go."* (the plan). Plan: `tests/docs/PLAN-208-371-BOARD-HOLDS-ITS-LAYOUT-AT-CLOSE-2026-10-12.md`.

**6:09 PM, verbatim:** *"1-nothing moves.  2-good.  3-go"* — (1) the Freeze holds the layout too; (2) the switch's note, his sentence: *"When bidding closes, the board keeps this layout until the next phase or round begins."*; (3) GO.

**Built as staff 208 / admin 371 (BUILD-LOG row 208).** One addition beyond the plan, told to him: the saved layout is written by EVERY admin lock write that leaves every week locked (not only Lock-every-week and Lock All), so locking the last weeks by hand or by Smart Lock cannot jump the board either.

**Put to him, UNRULED:** the review found that an admin UNLOCKING a week by hand after the close re-arranges the board (a deliberate admin act, like flipping the switch) — keep (Claude's recommendation) or hold the layout even then?

**FILED 12 Oct 2026 about 7:30 PM. PUSHED BY HIM about 7:50 PM — LIVE `ab7f2d8`**, served twice, the pushed pages md5-identical to the gated copies (tests `17ba1b7`, hub `7621028`).
