## §272 — OWNER-FOUND: PHASE 3 RESULTS E-MAILED TWICE TO EVERY DOCTOR — TWO SENDS OVERLAPPED; FIXED AS ADMIN 375, LIVE — 13 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 13 Oct 2026 V2".** 6:19 PM, verbatim: *"I'm also interested in removing the extra send phase results button in the messaging users card. It was used today by an admin other than myself to send results, and then I had to send results a second time to advance through the current phase card. any utility for that button anymore"* — then *"It was all phase 3 results"* and *"I actually don't know which send results button the other admin used"*, with two E-mail Log exports (failed-only; then the Phase 3 results kind — 88 rows).

**Read (code, live admin 374 `de3fc98`):** the Messaging Users card's "📮 Send Phase Results" and the Current Phase card's button call the SAME `sendPhaseResultsEmails()`; the phase card offers it only after Complete Phase (and the Begin Phase dialog warns if unsent); the Messaging button is always visible and its one extra use is e-mailing a doctor whose address was added after the next phase began. It is the only phase-step button with a second, always-visible copy (class hunt: Complete Phase, Begin Phase, round sends, skip, finish stamp — one place each).

**What the log shows (described by shape — the repos are public):** every one of the 44 results addresses received the Phase 3 results e-mail TWICE (88 rows). One send began about 9:28 AM and ran in bursts with ~8-minute pauses, finishing about 10:21 AM — the shape of a browser that kept sleeping (a locked phone or a background tab); a second send began about 9:41 AM and covered everyone in about a minute. The two e-mails were identical (the frozen Phase 3 record). The button used does not matter — both run the same send.

**Cause, as read in the code:** (1) the delivered-address ledger (`_writeSentLedger`) is written once, at the END of a send — a send started while another is still running reads it empty and mails everyone, and the first send, waking, carries on through the rest of the list; (2) the cross-session claim (`resultsSendClaim`) expires after 2 minutes, while a send can take far longer (53 minutes here); (3) the claim compares each browser's own clock. The Phase 4 round sender (`sendRoundResultsEmails`) has the same shape. Admin-page E-mail Log rows do not say which admin's page sent them (staff rows do), which is why the two sends cannot be attributed.

**Plan put to him (admin page only), NOT approved, nothing built (§92):** (1) record each address as delivered the moment its e-mail goes out, and re-read the server's ledger before each e-mail; (2) the claim held for as long as the send keeps progressing (renewed each e-mail), each e-mail preceded by a check that this send still holds it — a woken browser that lost the claim STOPS; (3) admin-page E-mail Log rows carry the sending admin's initials; (4) the same fix in the round sender; the bid-reminder send checked for the same shape; (5) remove the Messaging Users card's "Phase results" row (his ask). Claude's advice: build it in a fresh session (this one at about 457k) — it changes how results e-mails are sent and needs the full gate and an independent review; Phase 3 is closed, so it can land calmly before Phase 4.

**13 Oct 2026, session "Vacation Auction 13 Oct 2026 V3" — the plan re-read on live admin 374 and put to him as ONE plan (`tests/docs/PLAN-375-RESULTS-SENT-ONCE-2026-10-13.md`, admin 375). His answers 8:55 PM, verbatim: *"1-agree.  2-ok.  3-ok."*** — (1) bid reminders keep no guard between admins (left as is); (2) an admin not on the roster is shown on the E-mail Log by the name part of their Google address; (3) the Messaging Users "Phase results" row is removed, its one rare use (a past phase's results to a late-added address) given up. NOT yet a go to build. Same message, a NEW IDEA (filed in TODO §1, not part of 375 unless he says so): *"i'm also interested in maybe adding a between phases status to the auction since it can sit in that state for days-months sometimes.  this would be after phase compelte and before phase begin."* 8:56 PM, his clarification: *"admin site is currently says \"begin phase 4\".  user site says phase 3 complete.  i would like an in between state."* **8:56 PM, HIS GO on admin 375, verbatim: *"go375"*** — the in-between state to be explored after 375 is filed.

**Built 13 Oct 2026 (V3), his Mac away — admin 375.** As planned, with two changes from the independent review, told to him: (a) **no device's clock is compared with another's** — the claim carries a heartbeat; another page takes over only after watching it stand still for 2 minutes of its OWN clock, so taking over a dead send needs two clicks 2+ minutes apart (the first says "try again in 2 minutes") — the plan's line that a wrong clock "can now only cause an early handover, never a double" was WRONG (a clock ~90 s fast could take over a live send and double one address) and is withdrawn; (b) the refusal no longer quotes seconds, and the stop reads *"This page stopped sending Phase N results — another admin's page took over (or the send was reset) while this one was paused. Nothing more is sent from here."* Record `tests/docs/REVIEW-375-2026-10-13.md`; BUILD-LOG row 375.

## §276 — AN ADMIN SECTION FOR SENDING E-MAILS TO THE GROUP OR SELECTED USERS, ONE-TIME OR PERIODIC — 14 Oct 2026

Every e-mail goes through ONE EmailJS template whose SUBJECT is fixed in the template (the body is the `changes` parameter) — a custom subject needs a second template made in his EmailJS dashboard. ## §279 — AFTER THE SECURITY BUILD: STOPPING THE WORST KIND OF BAIL ON A COMPETITION WEEK, AND A "MINIMUM BID" LABEL — 14 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 14 Oct 2026 V2", 2:07 PM, verbatim:** *"after security build, i want to explore: Ways to make the worst kind of bailing impossible, not just tracked and Limit when a winning bid can be cancelled on a competition week.  A new idea I have is that instead of blocking someone leaving a comp week while winning, perhaps if they do leave, the engine could run a process/formula to determine what the lowest bid would have been without that user and that could become the floor so that users could potentially lower a bit since there is less competition.  I definitely want to add a minimum bid required label next to or below the competition week label to help guide users.  I am still undecided, it's possible that blocking a winning user from leaving a comp week is the better fix.  Please provide your opinion and pluses/minuses on which fix is better.  Handoff now and we can do the security build and then the comp week issue."*

**ORDER (his):** the site-security build (§275, with the results sender's gap folded in) FIRST, in a fresh session; THEN explore this. **RULED:** a "minimum bid required" label next to or below the "Competition week" label (*"I definitely want"*) — the level a new bid there must meet (§249 / §254). **UNDECIDED, his:** (A) block a winning bidder from leaving a competition week — his §253 idea, unless the number is needed on another of their weeks; or (B) his new idea — when a winner leaves, the engine works out what the lowest winning bid would have been without that person and makes it the floor, so the others may come down. Both NOT explored, NOT planned, nothing built (§92).

**Claude's view as given to him (2:1x PM), for the exploration to test, not a ruling:** A is the better main fix — it prevents rather than repairs; it is one rule a colleague can read; the "needed elsewhere" test already exists and is tested in the Fair Play report's Bail / Fair — needed logic (§263); a small build. Its costs: it can trap someone whose plans really change (an admin removal on request is the escape hatch); the "needed" exception can be gamed, but only by really losing a week elsewhere. B does not deter (the bid-up-then-leave still happens and still moves people's other bids in the meantime); its number is a counterfactual — bids made in reaction to the leaver cannot be undone, so "without that user" is a replay, not the truth, and hard to explain to 35 colleagues; it is a large engine change (change-log replay across returns, Under Review, approvals and Phase 4 rounds) with a heavy audit; and lowering by others sets off alert e-mails. B could later complement A for the cases A lets through. The label helps under either.


**15 Oct 2026, session "Vacation Auction 15 Oct 2026 V1" — EXPLORED (read-only, live `1a47c63`); plan `tests/docs/PLAN-214-MIN-BID-LABEL-AND-NO-BAIL-2026-10-15.md`; NOT approved, nothing built.** **CLAUDE CORRECTS ITS 14 OCT VIEW, ON EVIDENCE:** it said A was small because the report's "needed elsewhere" test already exists. The report judges where the number WENT, which is known only afterwards; at the moment of leaving the page can only ask "knocked out of winning somewhere?", and the 11 Oct look-back (`PLAN-366` §2) found that true for all 14 real leaves, the 3 bails included — a plain "block unless needed" would have stopped none of them. A is still recommended, in a different shape: a winning bid on a competition week cannot be removed, only MOVED in one step to a week where its owner was knocked out of winning by someone else and is not winning now (the report's "Fair — needed" made a requirement); medium-sized. B not recommended (it does not deter; its number cannot be worked out truthfully; it loosens a rule). The label is a separate small build. Seven questions put to him.

**15 Oct 2026, 7:01 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"Move, not remove is good.  What happens to the bid on the other week?  It gets put back to available at the same time?  3 - This: Your report's own test: knocked out of winning there by someone else this phase or round, and not winning there now."* **So: A as "move, not remove" (question 2); the destination test is the report's own (question 3).** Answer given to his question: if the person still has a bid on the week the number moves to, the moved number replaces it in the same save, and the old bid's number(s) are back in their available numbers at that moment. Questions 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 still open. Not a go to build.

**15 Oct 2026, 7:02 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"Agree with all the others"* — questions 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 as put in the plan. Read as: (1) "Minimum bid: 5" under "Competition week", the same for everyone, hidden when unknown or NP, in the bid box too; (4) a winning NP on a competition week is held like any winning bid — the admin removes it on request; (5) when the page cannot tell, Remove works as today; (6) the wordings of the plan's §6; (7) two builds — the label first (staff 214), then move-not-remove. Not yet a go to build.

**15 Oct 2026, 7:04 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"An NP can always be removed.  2 builds is good.  Go with label."* — question 4 CORRECTED: a winning NP may always be removed (move-not-remove applies to numbered bids only); two builds confirmed; **GO on build 1, the "Minimum bid" label (staff 214).**

**15 Oct 2026, 9:22–9:33 AM (V1), while staff 214 was in its gates — HIS, verbatim:** *"If we pursue the comp week restrictions, I am wondering if we no longer need a bid lowering restriction?  Is it a bid lowering restriction that prevents a user who left a week from coming back with a lower bid that meets the minimum?"* · *"I also want to know if there are backdoors around the new comp week rule about leaving.  For instance could User A have a Bid 1 and be winning Wk 4, become a projected loser of Wk 5 with their Bid 7, then move their Bid 1 to Wk 5, lower to a 7 and free up their 1?"* · on the review's note that a returning bidder's box can be stricter than the shared "Minimum bid" number: *"This needs to be solved"* — **RULED: the label must not show a number the viewer's own box refuses.** Answers and the proposed fix (a personal "Minimum bid") put to him the same morning; not yet a go.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:41 AM (V1) — HIS, verbatim:** *"Okay, so the user moves their 1 to Wk 5, then drops to a 5, which would still win.  could that be a way to free up the #1 bid?  Also, I'm not that concerned about a user scaring people off with a high bid early since someone could still put in a 10 and be winning despite another user's 1."* Claude's answer: yes, that route frees the 1 (a weakening that still wins is allowed; today it costs one bid lowering) — proposed for the move-not-remove build: a MOVED number is final on the week it moved to for the rest of the phase (in Phase 4, the round): no lowering, removal or swap-down of it there, stronger still allowed. On the bid-lowering lock: Claude agrees it may be redundant once move-not-remove ships (on a week with room for everyone a bid's strength changes nobody's outcome; competition weeks have their own rules) — to be decided in that build's plan, with every place the lock is used checked. Not yet a go on either.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:47 AM (V1) — HIS, verbatim:** *"I'm still not sure about the personal minimum.  Is it 100% necessary?  I am also not convinced that your move-not-remove fix is good.  As things keep changing, it could be possible a bid that was moved once needs to be moved again.  It's quite complex with all the possibilities.  Think hard and consider all options for this solution.  I want people to bid honestly and not be able to drive up the cost for a week if their not 100% commited.  I also need to leave open the option to shift and respond to other unexpected changes."* — his three goals for any fix: honest bidding, no driving up a week without commitment, room to shift. **Move-not-remove (7:01 AM) is RE-OPENED, not withdrawn.** Claude's answer (options weighed; real data from his 15 Oct backup — 15 winning exits by 11 people across Phases 1–3: per person per phase 10 × one, 1 × two, 1 × three): recommended an EXIT ALLOWANCE — removing a WINNING bid from a competition week uses one bid lowering (or its own per-phase count), any number may move any time within it, every route out costs the same so the "move then lower" route needs no special rule; and for the label, "Minimum new bid: 5" (one shared number, true for everyone) instead of a personal minimum. Not yet ruled.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:50 AM (V1) — DECLINED, verbatim:** *"Exit allowance is a terrible idea"* — the exit allowance (a winning exit from a competition week costs a bid lowering or its own count) is NOT to be proposed again. The bail fix stays open: move-not-remove re-opened (9:47), his three goals stand.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:51 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"I want 0 allowances for things like this"* — no budget, count or allowance of any kind may be the mechanism for bailing or leaving a competition week; the rule itself must decide. Claude's direction for the fresh-session plan (not yet ruled): the "needed" test with no "final" state (a moved number can move again whenever its owner is knocked out somewhere else), and a number freed from a winning competition-week bid by any route usable only on a week where its owner was knocked out of winning (closes the 9:41 "move the 1, drop to a 5" route).

**15 Oct 2026, 9:51 AM (V1), his reason, verbatim:** *"It's bad because I don't want a single instance of bailing allowed that isn't in good faith because a bid is needed elsewhere"* — the standard for the bail fix: ZERO bails; the only leave from a winning competition-week bid is a good-faith one, the number needed elsewhere.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:53 AM (V1) — HIS IDEA, verbatim:** *"What if a user who moved that 1 from a winning position for another week then couldn't lower at all?  they had to use the 1."* Claude: better than its own "freed number" rule — narrower (ordinary still-winning lowerings elsewhere stay free), closes the move-then-lower route, and keeps moving again possible. For the fresh-session plan: a moved bid cannot be weakened on the week it moved to (by Edit or Swap) for the rest of the phase / round; it may be strengthened, or moved again to another week where its owner was knocked out of winning; if it is later knocked out there and is losing, it may be removed like any losing bid. Not yet a go.

**15 Oct 2026, 9:53 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"There would need to be a warning about that"* — the move step must warn, before the move is confirmed, that the moved bid cannot be lowered on the week it moves to for the rest of the phase (round). Draft wording put to him: "Once moved, this bid can't be lowered on Week N for the rest of this phase. You can still raise it, or move it again if you are knocked out of winning on another week."

**15 Oct 2026, 10:00–10:10 AM (V1) — RULED for staff 214:** finish 214 in this session; the bail fix is a new session (*"i want to finish 214 here and then the bail fix is a new session"*). His idea, 10:06: label "Minimum new bid" on the card, and the bid box explains a returner's own, stricter minimum. Wordings 1 (already on the week) and 2 (coming back) of the bid-box note: *"1 and 2 are perfect."* — "Your minimum bid here is N. You already have a B on this competition week. You can lower it only if it would still win and is no lower than the lowest other winning bid, so N is the lowest you can go. The "Minimum new bid" of L is for people who have not bid on this week this phase." / "Your minimum bid here is N. You bid B on this competition week earlier this phase. A bid weaker than that is allowed only if it would still win and is no lower than the lowest other winning bid, so N is the lowest you can bid. The "Minimum new bid" of L is for people who have not bid on this week this phase." **His lean, verbatim:** *"I lean towards removing bid lowering restrictions if we add this new comp week rule that restricts lowering/leaving.  I just don't think we need bid lowering restrictions anymore with this in place.  Correct me if I am wrong."* — Claude's correction: one case remains, a week with only one person's worth of room left (common in Phases 3–4): a 1 there blocks everyone but a 1/2 or 1/2/3, nobody else bids so it never becomes a competition week, and without the lock its holder could drop to a 10 near the end; to be decided in the bail-fix plan.

**15 Oct 2026, 10:13 AM (V1) — RULED, verbatim:** *"I prefer to figure out a way to remove bid lowerings as we have them now. If you rpefer to build them and just be unlikely to ever use them, also okay with me if it's cheap to do.,"* — the bail-fix session is to find a way to REMOVE bid lowerings as they are now, closing first the one-room-left loophole Claude raised (a 1 that keeps others off a week that never becomes a competition week, dropped near the end), then hunting other loopholes. 214 leaves out the bid-lowering note (wording 3 and the lowering line); where the lock alone sets a person's limit, the note is not shown rather than say something untrue.

**15 Oct 2026, 10:15 AM (V1) — HIS CONCERN, verbatim, for the bail-fix session:** *"A new concern I just realized is that if bailing was not allowed, would that make people slower to raise bids and delay things?  If so, I need ideas to prevent that."* Claude's first answer given in the chat (to be tested on his data in that session): the rule removes uncommitted bidding wars, which are what cause most of the churn and alerts; the move rule leaves a committed bidder a way to respond, so the commitment is less of a gamble; waiting already has a cost, since bidding closes when the countdown runs out; and how late raises actually came in Phases 1–3 can be measured from the change log before any further idea is chosen.

## §280 — THE SHORT START MESSAGE: START-HERE IS READ FROM THE DISK, NEVER PASTED, AND NO LONGER GOES TO THE OUTPUTS COLUMN — 15 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 15 Oct 2026 V2", about 11:42–11:45 AM.** His questions, verbatim: *"First, can you always read start here from the disk instead of the chat?  Can we make a shorter start here that directs each session to one on the disk?  Would that be better anyway so it's read twice?"* and, correcting the last word, *"not read twice"*. Claude's answer: yes — the pasted copy goes stale (that morning's paste was older than the disk, V1's close having edited it after he copied it, and the session had followed the pasted rules — what START-HERE rule 13 forbids); a paste plus a disk read costs about 45,000 tokens twice; and a short message holding no dates or build numbers never goes stale. Proposed: the short message, kept in `SESSION-START.md`; if the Mac cannot be reached the session says so and stops rather than work from an older copy (GitHub's copy lacks unpushed edits).

**His rulings, verbatim:** *"1-yes stop that."* — START-HERE is no longer delivered to the outputs column at the start and end of a session (§212 SUPERSEDED). And: *"I also want you to always present the new, shortened start here message with the commit lines within the chat so it's easy to copy and start the next session.  Ensure that's remembered."* — at every hand-over, and whenever commit lines are given, the message from `SESSION-START.md` goes IN THE CHAT in its own copy-box right after the commit lines. Recorded in START-HERE (the line under the LIVE line, §3's paperwork table and commit paragraph, §4 step 5), HANDOFF's hand-over step, and his saved preferences. START-HERE itself is still never pasted into the chat (§247 stays withdrawn).

**Asked, not yet ruled:** shortening START-HERE's LIVE line to the current builds and baseline (the history is in each BUILD-LOG).

**Narrowed the same day, 12:01 PM, verbatim:** *"next session start message should only be displayed when handoff routine is performed."* — the short start message goes in the chat at the hand-over (the closing checklist) only; mid-session commit lines go in the chat without it.

## §281 — START-HERE'S LIVE LINE SHORTENED TO THE CURRENT BUILDS AND BASELINE — 15 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 15 Oct 2026 V2", 11:51 AM.** Asked under §280 whether to shorten START-HERE's LIVE line — one paragraph that had grown a clause for every build since staff 192, about 17,700 characters, roughly 16% of the file and about 7,000 tokens read every session. Claude measured it and put the downsides: nothing is lost (the old text moves verbatim to `START-HERE-ARCHIVE.md`, and each build is already its BUILD-LOG row); `status.mjs` reads only the build numbers at the line's start, so the gate is unaffected; an older commit or md5 costs one search of BUILD-LOG. His ruling, verbatim: *"yes, shorten"*. The line now carries the current builds, their commits and the honesty baseline only; a session that ships a build REPLACES those, never appends (START-HERE's LIVE line and HANDOFF step 2 say so).

## §282 — RULE SETS THE ADMIN CAN SWITCH: THE MENU TAKES SHAPE — 15 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 15 Oct 2026 V2", 11:56 AM – 12:04 PM. An idea in shaping — NOT explored, NOT planned, nothing built (§92).** His idea (TODO §1, verbatim there): the admin chooses among a few SETS of rules governing how users place, edit and cancel bids, so the group can pivot later; he is *"not entirely certain"* he wants the bail fix. At 11:57: *"I'm imagining 2-4 sets of rules that would govern how users can place/edit/cancel bids with varying degrees of restrictions"*. Claude put a ladder: Open · Standard (live today) · Strict (Standard + a winning competition-week bid can only move, not be removed) · Strictest (Strict + no lowering).

**His rulings, 12:04 PM, verbatim:** *"Open doesn't work.  We tried that and won't try it again.  I guess I only need 2 options at this time, since I don't strictest adds anything.  Is there a different 3rd option I could consider?  I think we could have Standard and Strict at the minimum.  I'm still not sure how to handle the currently built bid lowering restrictions ... they don't seem to add much once we tighten comp week change rules"* — Open DECLINED; Strictest dropped; Standard and Strict at the minimum; a third option asked for; the lowering restrictions undecided.

**Claude's answer the same turn (to be confirmed in the code when explored):** under Strict, lowering is the back door — a winner who cannot remove a competition-week bid could still hold a strong number to keep others off and weaken it near the end, freeing that number; so Strict should forbid weakening a bid on a competition week as well as removing it (it may only MOVE), and Standard keeps today's lowering lines unchanged. The one-room-left case (a strong bid keeping others off a week that never becomes a competition week) is outside both sets as they stand. Third-option ideas put to him: a late lock (Standard until the last hours of a phase, Strict in them), or "leave and stay out" (leaving a winning competition-week bid allowed, but no bid on that week again this phase).

**His rulings, 12:09 PM, verbatim:** *"a single person bidding a 1 on a week with 4 spots doesn't scare anyone off.  3 other people can bid 10 and they all win.  I'm okay with that 1 being lowered to a 10.  On all of these options, users can only lower to the lowest winning bid already and that should always be okay.  We need to solve the gap you mention, that's a real problem.  2 sets for now."* — TWO sets for now, Standard and Strict; the lowering rules stay as live today in BOTH (lowering only while still winning and no lower than the lowest other winning bid); the gap must be solved. **Claude withdrew its 12:04 "lowering is the back door" claim on his reasoning (15 Oct 2026, 12:09 PM):** a strong bid on a week that is not full deters no one, and on a competition week a newcomer is measured against the weakest winning bid, not the strongest, so a bid lowered no further than that takes nothing from anyone. **The gap, as Claude restated it for the plan:** it is a FULL week, not only one room left — a week can be full (as many bids as places, every one winning) without ever becoming a competition week, which needs MORE bids than places; strong bids filling it can keep others off, and nothing stops them leaving or dropping late. One room left with a single 1 is its simplest case.

**His rulings, 12:13 PM, verbatim:** *"1-yes, that's it.  2- gap fix applies to both.  I wonder if we can leave bid lowerings as they are, but default them all to off.  I would want the rules section to drop all mention of bid lowerings when all phases are toggled to off.  When 1 or more phases is turned on, the rule should specify each phase as I think it currently does.  I'm not sure if treating a full week as a comp week is the right answer.  what other options are there?"* — the full-week gap as restated is the gap; its fix applies in BOTH sets; Bid Lowerings kept as built, off by default, no mention of them in the rules when every phase is off. **What Claude read in the live code the same turn (staff 214 / admin 379):** `prioLockBullets()` already prints only the priority-lock sentence when every phase's allowance is 0 and names each phase when any is on; an unset allowance reads as 0; the allowance is frozen once Phase 1 begins and SURVIVES the admin's new-auction reset (admin, the reset's comment: "The ALLOWANCE … survives reset like the floors"). Not yet covered: the competition-week rule line (`#ruleNoWeaken`, static text) still says when a bid may be weakened. Options for the gap put to him: a full week treated as a competition week; a late lock keyed to the countdown; leaving re-opens time; a losing bid already makes the week a competition week (the cheapest).

**His idea, 12:16 PM, verbatim:** *"I'm thinking that there would be real risk to a user placing a 1 they don't intend to use on a week.  a 1.0 FTE bidding a 1 on a week with 1 spot available is at high risk of getting locked in.  I think another user could place an NP bid on that week and be under review or losing, but also make it so that the other user couldn't lower below a 1.  Is that tight?  That's a pretty strict consequence."* **Claude's reading of live staff 214 the same turn:** `_wwContested` counts any current-phase bid, NP included, by FTE (two people at least, their FTE over the places), and a week stays a competition week for the rest of the phase; NP is allowed by default only in Phases 3 and 4 (`npAllowedNow`, unless the admin's NP phases say otherwise) and is refused on a week with a bid floor; an NP counts toward the running bid cap. **Claude's answer:** the idea protects the person who wants the week in BOTH sets (if the 1 leaves, their NP inherits it); the lock-in is Strict's alone; the holes are (1) anyone, wanting the week or not, can lock a rival's strong bid at no lasting cost — bid NP, remove it, and the week stays a competition week all phase; (2) NP is not available in Phases 1–2 by default or on floored (summer / highest-demand) weeks, where a real number must be spent; (3) half-time people: two at 0.5 on a one-place week are not over, so a 0.5 holder cannot be caught by a 0.5.

**His thought, 12:21 PM, verbatim:** *"Re: the holes.  I wonder if there's an option to allow a comp week to not be a comp week anymore if certain conditions are met.  I don't like the idea that someone could weaponize their NP bid.  However, people should only be placing bids they intend to keep, so maybe not worth worrying about."* **Claude's options the same turn:** (1) keep it simple — a competition week stays one all phase, as today; the weapon only hurts someone holding a bid they no longer want, which Strict already asks people not to do; the Fair Play report could LIST a quick add-then-remove that turned a week into a competition week, so the admin sees it (a report, not a rule) — Claude's recommendation; (2) short stays do not count — a bid removed within some hours of being placed never makes a competition week for the lock (stops hit-and-run; a real bidder who leaves quickly loses the protection; the label could appear and then vanish); (3) a competition week ends after some hours without being over-full — advised AGAINST: it reopens the main bail (a winner waits for the others to leave, then leaves).

**His question, 12:26 PM, verbatim:** *"Question.  I think the new bidder placing the bid that turns the week into a competition week has restrictions already.  Can that new bidder currnently place a bid that would be under review or losing?  If they can't place that bid, that can't do the hit and run."* **Answer, read in live staff 214 the same turn — he is right:** `_weBlocks` judges a newcomer on the picture WITH their bid; if that bid makes the week a competition week it must be at least as strong as the weakest bid there that is winning, a draw or under review, or it is refused. Every choice in the bid box passes through `_wwRefuses` (NP included — `priorityScore('NP')` is 99, the weakest), and again at review and at confirm. So a cheap NP cannot make a full week a competition week; a hit-and-run costs a number at least as strong as the holder's. **Claude corrected its 12:16 answer (15 Oct 2026, 12:26 PM):** it had read only the counting (`_wwContested`), not the entry rule — the NP "next in line" protection (option D) does not exist either, so the weapon hole is closed and the full-week gap is open again without its cheap fix. Remaining: a late lock on full weeks keyed to the countdown; a full week counted as a competition week; a late leave restarting the countdown (whether a removal restarts it depends on the admin's timer mode — to be read). In Standard, leaving a competition week is free today, so a full-week fix there either covers competition weeks too (Standard gains a late lock) or Standard keeps the gap — his call.

**His ruling, 12:27 PM, verbatim:** *"This doesn't address the problem of a user placing a high bid that discourages others from bidding and then lowering last minute.  That's a real loophole that needs fixing."* — the LOWERING route on a full week is a real loophole and must be fixed. **Claude's reading (live staff 214):** with the priority lock on and every phase's lowering allowance 0, no bid can be lowered anywhere (`blExhaustedMsg`: "can only stay the same or get stronger"), and a remove-then-weaker-re-bid is held to the remembered best bid — so under his "lowerings off" default the lowering route is already shut; it opens in a phase where the admin grants lowerings, because the competition-week lowering limit (`_wwRefuses`: still winning, no lower than the lowest other winning bid) applies only on competition weeks (`if(!_wwContested(wk)) return false`). On a full week the deterrent a newcomer meets is the WEAKEST winning bid (the level they must match), so a strong bid lowered no further than the lowest other winning bid changes nothing; the harm is the bid that SETS that level dropping. **Claude's proposal:** apply the competition-week lowering limit to full weeks too, in both sets — his own 12:09 principle ("users can only lower to the lowest winning bid already and that should always be okay"); what it does for a holder alone on a one-place week (no other winning bid) to be confirmed in `_wwBelow`. Removal from a full week (a bail) is a separate question.

**CORRECTION, his words, 12:30 PM, verbatim:** *"Oops.  I never meant lowerings off as default.  I intended to say lowerings unrestricted is the new default.  You should have known that's what I intended to say from our conversation."* — his 12:13 "default them all to off" meant the lowering RESTRICTIONS off: the new default is lowerings UNRESTRICTED in every phase, and the rules section says nothing about lowering limits when every phase is unrestricted; when one or more phases is restricted, the rule names each phase. Claude's 12:13 and 12:27 statements that "lowerings off" was his default are WRONG and superseded by this line (Claude had read "them" as the lowerings, against his 12:09 "I'm okay with that 1 being lowered to a 10"). Consequence: under the new default the full-week lowering route is OPEN, so the 12:27 fix (the competition-week lowering limit applied to full weeks, in both sets) is needed by default, not only in phases with lowerings granted; that limit is separate code from the lowering allowance and still applies when lowerings are unrestricted.

**His finding, 12:38 PM, verbatim:** *"Your full week doesn't fix a .9 FTE in a week with 1 FTE available lowering last minute"* — right: 0.9 of 1.0 is not full, yet any colleague above 0.1 FTE would push it over and meet the entry level, so the wall stands and "full" missed it. **Claude's corrected test (12:38 PM, superseding "full week" in the 12:27 and 12:35 entries):** the lowering limit applies on a week where the room left is smaller than some colleague's FTE — where at least one colleague's arrival would make it a competition week and so meet a minimum; there a winning bid may be lowered no further than the lowest other winning bid, and not at all when alone. **BUILD 1 — TIES, 16 Oct 2026, 5:57 AM (session "Vacation Auction 16 Oct 2026 V1").** Claude, putting the redrafted wording to him, said a cooperating colleague could match a winner's bid to make a tie "and then both can leave freely", and asked whether the Fair Play report should list tie and under-review exits. **He asked, verbatim:** *"Is this true?"* · *"if one left, then the other would be winning and be stuck?"* · *"a tie is a legit way out, not a loophole."* **Claude's correction (re-read in live staff 215 the same turn, rule 14):** a matching bid is allowed (`_weBlocks` refuses only a WEAKER bid than the level); a tie is a draw only when the tied group straddles the edge of the room (`computeApprovals`, `anyFits`); once one of the two leaves, the other is alone in that place and is WINNING — under Strict, held. Only ONE can leave, and the one left behind is the colleague who made the tie. Under build 1 the removal is judged again on the server's copy at the moment of saving, so two clicks in the same second cannot both get out. "Both can leave freely" was WRONG. **Ruled:** a tie is a legitimate way out (confirms his 2:07 PM ruling of 15 Oct). **Then, 5:58 AM, verbatim:** *"2-yes"* — to question 2 (keep the ruling, AND the Fair Play report lists tie and under-review exits on competition weeks so he can see them): read by Claude as YES to the listing — shown as information, never as a Bail — and put back to him to confirm, since it came a minute after "not a loophole". ## §283 — ADMIN SETTINGS REMEMBERED AS THEY LAST WERE; NO DEFAULTS — 15 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 15 Oct 2026 V2", 12:52 PM. His want, verbatim:** *"I actually want to remove all default settings and have all admin controlled settings be remembered as they last were.  I think this mainly applies to auction resets, is that right?"*

**Claude's reading of live admin 379 the same turn:** every write to `vacations/adminSettings` is a merge, and only three code paths change a setting the admin did not just set: the new-auction reset (`fteEditEnabled:false`), Begin Phase 1 (`fteEditEnabled:false`; Rehearsal Mode `simulatorEnabled:false` before a real launch), and the reset's timer document (off, 48 h, `biddingClosed:false` — phase state rather than a setting). Everything else — bid floors, Bid Lowerings, NP phases, bid caps, the review threshold, timer rules and reset mode, blinded bidding, locked weeks in place, the e-mail switches — already survives a reset. The staff and mobile pages never write the document. Built-in defaults (NP in Phases 3–4, lowering allowance 0, the priority lock on, and so on) apply only to a setting that has never been saved. Claude recommended keeping the three guards (FTE editing re-locked and Rehearsal Mode off protect real data at the start of an auction). Awaiting his answer.

**His ruling, 12:58 PM, verbatim:** *"Timer at 48 hrs isn't right, the other 2 are okay.  file the timer default setting to fix later."* — the FTE-editing and Rehearsal Mode guards stay; the reset's 48-hour timer is wrong and is filed to fix later (TODO, tabled).

## §286 — STRICT ONLY FOR WINNING BIDS OF N OR STRONGER (adminSettings.strictThreshold) — 17 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 17 Oct 2026 V1".** His idea of 16 Oct 2026 12:27 PM (verbatim in `TODO.md` §1, NEXT ITEM 2); explored read-only 17 Oct 2026 (session "Vacation Auction 16 Oct 2026 V2") as ONE plan, `tests/docs/PLAN-STRICT-ABOVE-N-2026-10-17.md`, 14 questions (§13). Put to him in plain words at 7:07 AM.

**His answers, 7:15 AM, verbatim:** *"agree with all, except: 3-choices are only the whole numbers, not combos. 4-is this basically that a default would be that strict rule applies to all bids?  5-but they can only lower to other lowest winning bid.  if that other winning bid is 8, then i'm okay with it.  correct?"* — RULED: Q1, Q2, Q6–Q14 as recommended (build it as its own small build; n or stronger held, 4/5 stronger than 5; NP unchanged; Fair Play judged as under Standard; frozen like the rule set, RA-2 and rules first; the admin row always shown; the ordinary Remove box; the Confirm note for n or stronger only; the phone test page before any code, where the wording W1–W12 is judged; the runbook line). **Q3:** the choices are the WHOLE numbers only, never a combination — "Every numbered bid" and 9 … 1 (as the plan already had it; told to him). **Q4:** asked, answered by Claude the same turn: yes — never set = Strict holds every winning numbered bid on a competition week, exactly as now (NP, ties, under review and losing bids free, as now). **Q5 OPEN:** he would allow the two-step route when the other winning bid there is weak (his example 8). Claude read `_wwRefuses` / `_wwBelow` (staff 217, index.html 3632, 3795–3816) and told him: a winning bid may be lowered to ANY bid that still wins and is no weaker than the weakest OTHER winning bid of the phase there — not only to exactly that bid — so from a 3 beside a winning 8 it may go to 4 … 8; at n = 5 anything from 6 to 8 is then free to remove. Without the closure, Strict above n holds a strong bid only on competition weeks where EVERY other winning bid is n or stronger. Claude's recommendation unchanged (the closure); his choice awaited. Nothing built (§92).

**Q5 RULED 7:20 AM, verbatim:** *"B for sure."* — and the same minute: *"think about it more.  is this not consistent with what i've said in ther past?"* — **NO CLOSURE (S4 and S7 dropped).** A winning bid of n or stronger on a competition week may be lowered by the ordinary rules (§248: still winning, no weaker than the weakest other winning bid of the phase there; a lowering spent below the floor); once it is weaker than n it is no longer held and may be removed. **Claude's position changed on evidence (17 Oct 2026, 7:21 AM), said to him:** B is two of his own rulings working together — §248 deliberately allows lowering to the week's weakest other winner while still winning, and his 16 Oct idea frees a winning bid weaker than n. It is NOT the exit allowance declined in §279 (leaving a strong winning bid directly, paid for with a lowering or a count): here the strong bid must first come down to the level the week is actually being won at, where it was not needed. Claude's "close to the exit allowance" framing at 7:15 AM was wrong. Wording consequence: the plan's W5 and W6 (the refusal and its bid-box note) are dropped, and the "can't be lowered below n" clauses leave W3, W7, W8 and W9 (plan §6b). Nothing built (§92).

RULED (plan §11m):
- In the alert window's step 1, a week that is locked right now cannot be chosen. It keeps its *🔒 Locked* tag; the line above the buttons reads *This week is locked right now.* The "only if it opens again" note is dropped.

Claude had recommended yes and withdrew it the same turn, on the code: weeks lock and unlock only when the admin acts (admin 383: Smart Lock, the per-week locks, the Freeze, a phase close or run-out), a user cannot change a bid on a locked week, and the alert ends with the phase or round — so a locked week's alert would almost never be sent. No open questions remain on the alerts plan. NOT built (§289).

## §290 — BUILDS FASTER: THE FULL BATTERY ONLY AT THE GATES, SHORT FUZZ WHILE BUILDING, SUITES TWO AT A TIME — 21 Oct 2026

**Session "Vacation Auction 21 Oct 2026 V1", 8:12 PM, during the alerts build (staff 220 / admin 384), verbatim:** *"My builds have been taking 4-8 hours lately.  Is this expected?  Are we doing this efficiently?  I like have having checks and tests done to make sure things are done well, but it seems like things are taking much longer than they did in the past.  What efficiencies can we achieve?"*

**What Claude measured and told him (the same turn):** one full auction battery in the cloud is 184–191 suites and about 26 minutes; part 1 of this build ran it three times (about 78 of its 85 minutes); four suites take 13 of the 27 minutes (`test-216-standard-invariance` alone 7); the battery has grown from about 116 suites (8 Oct) to 191; each build also writes an undo (`un<N>.mjs`) and rewires older test libraries, a cost that grows every build; the cloud machine has 2 processors. Proposed: (1) the FULL battery only at the gates — once when a build is complete, once after its fix round — and, while building, only the new suites and the suites touching the changed code; (2) reduced fuzz counts in those mid-build runs, full counts at the gates; (3) suites two at a time, trusted only after one run on unchanged bytes agrees with the one-at-a-time run; (4) later, his call: simplify the undo chain (freeze old invariance suites on their own copies) — not now; (5) NOT cut: the blind reviews and the verifier (they found real HIGHs in 192, 196, 200, 207).

**8:15 PM, verbatim:** *"yes, thanks"* — RULED: (1)–(3) for the rest of this build and as standing practice (START-HERE §6). (4) is an idea only, filed in TODO §1; nothing built.

**Result of (3), measured the same evening (~9:00 PM):** two at a time on the live 219 / 383 bytes matched the one-at-a-time baseline on 183 of 184 suites; the one difference was `test-216-standard-invariance` running past its 900-second limit. Wall time 23 minutes against 24 — no real gain, because the 2 processors were also busy with the builder and the reviewers. NOT adopted: the gates run one at a time. To re-check only on an otherwise idle machine, with the time limit raised. (1) and (2) stand.

## §291 — THE ALERTS BUILD (STAFF 220 / ADMIN 384): RULINGS DURING THE BUILD AND ITS REVIEWS — 21 Oct 2026

**His GO, 3:07 PM (session "Vacation Auction 21 Oct 2026 V1"), verbatim:** *"go"* — then *"mac will be away"*: the alerts build of `tests/docs/PLAN-ALERTS-2026-10-18.md` §10 and §11 whole, built in the cloud.

**The welcome at ▶ Begin Phase 1 in a rehearsal (review lane 3, M3).** Put to him: in a rehearsal, Begin Phase 1 sends the welcome to the whole roster unless the Welcome switch is off, and Reset Auction clears the welcome log, so the real start welcomes everyone again. **9:56 PM, verbatim:** *"In a rehearsal, I turn off the e-mail sends."* — RULED: no change; the Welcome switch and the dialog's count are the control.

**A slow save of an alert choice (review lane 3, M2).** Put to him: when a save of an alert choice has had no answer yet, the toast says *"Not confirmed yet — it will save when your connection returns."*; *"Could not save — try again in a moment."* stays for a refused save. **10:08 PM, verbatim:** *"wording is good"* — APPROVED.

**FILED 22 Oct 2026 about 3:10 AM, NOT PUSHED — staff 220 / admin 384 + `firestore.rules`** (gated staff `7330d1a5…`, admin `3b04191b…`, rules `1cc2d6ae…`, versions.json `{"index": 220, "mobile": 18, "admin": 384}`). Built in three parts (the rules wording, Part A / B removed, the welcome at ▶ Begin Phase 1, the `alertPrefs` rules clause · the 🔔 E-mail alerts box, the three-step alert window, the bell marker, the get-in alert, the confirmation e-mail, the admin's one-line view · the 60-second hold on a cancel's alerts); three blind review lanes (HIGHs: the bell clipped "Competition week" at 390 / 414 wide; a held e-mail could arrive after a newer one — both fixed); three fix rounds; three verifications — final verdict SAFE TO DEPLOY — yes. Final gate battery 192 suites, 189 passed, 3 skipped (the baseline's own), 0 red; test site republished (220 / 384). Record: `tests/docs/REVIEW-220-384-2026-10-22.md`, `tests/docs/build220/`, the BUILD-LOG row. **Push order (rules change): RA-2 → publish the rules in the console → push the pages; open the admin page right after the push (it reloads old open tabs).**

**Left for him (put to him at filing, not ruled):** (1) **A4** — when four changes hit the same week within about 5 seconds, one of them an admin edit, a MIDDLE e-mail of the four can arrive out of order; the last e-mail is always true. The verifier: the 60-second hold has converged on its main promise (no lost e-mail, no false last e-mail in any run), but each fix round added ordering machinery (about ten functions per page, coordinated by timestamps and timers on one shared queue with no server). The simpler design if he wants it: merge only a re-bid from the same page, and when a held e-mail is finally sent drop any line no longer true (costs "the same characters" for stale lines). (2) The 🔔 marker on the week card is small (about 9–11 px) — to look at on his phone on the test site.

**22 Oct 2026, 5:44 AM, verbatim:** *"I'm fine with the bell icon as it is.  When I added an alert, the e-mails appear to log correctly, although after adding an alert, it says nothing changed, no e-mail sent.  I always love simpler and i would prefer you present these ideas sooner rather than wait and build complexity that maybe is not even necessary.  re: the 60 second hold, I agree that only cancels from the same browser should be linked."*
- **The 🔔 marker — APPROVED as built.**
- **OWNER-FOUND on the test site (staff 220):** after an alert is added through the window, closing the 🔔 box says *"Done — nothing changed, no e-mail."* — the window had already sent the confirmation e-mail (it logged correctly), so the toast is untrue. Cause as read (staff `_alDone`): the window's own e-mail resets the visit's starting point, so Done sees no further change. Proposed fix (not ruled): show a toast at Done only when an e-mail goes (*"Done — a confirmation e-mail is on its way."*); otherwise close quietly.
- **RULED — THE 60-SECOND HOLD SIMPLIFIED:** only a cancel and a re-bid on the same week FROM THE SAME BROWSER are linked (merged). The cross-page ordering machinery added in the three fix rounds (held e-mails released before any newer alert, a page's own e-mails written first and ordered behind older ones) is to be taken out. Claude's simpler design put to him at filing and taken by his "I always love simpler": when a held e-mail is finally sent, any line that is no longer true is dropped (an e-mail with nothing left is not sent) — it costs "the same characters" only for stale lines. Plan in a fresh session.
- **STANDING (START-HERE rule 21): present the simpler option EARLY** — when a design starts to grow machinery to handle rare cases, stop and put the simpler version to him before building further.
- **Push held:** staff 220 / admin 384 is NOT to be pushed as filed; it is revised first (the simpler hold + the Done toast), re-gated, then pushed once (Claude's recommendation, 22 Oct — the site is idle for months, so one push is simpler than two).
